- *File keys are short lived (finding 14).* The file key in a manifest is kept in a separate transfer record, not in the archived manifest, and is erased as soon as the file is complete and verified, abandoned, withdrawn or expired; the vault has its own key. A later compromise of the archive then cannot decrypt captured segments.

The user decides Q10 with these tables; until then phase 3 is blocked.

**M10. Carriers (phase 3).** As Design A v2/v3 with these additions from the reference apps and the reviews. *Two admission paths (finding 7).* The recipient path is v1's: a header is accepted for chunks only after its slot authenticates a contact as the owner of the tag. The carrier path cannot do that (an opaque carrier holds no key) and is charged to the authenticated supplying neighbour instead: strict header decode, class 0 to 3, version 4 or 5 only, at most 256 chunks, every chunk checked against the hash list, expiry inside the allowed window, at most 10% of the carrier quota per tag and 25% per supplying neighbour, and the neighbour's own daily byte and bundle allowances (all non-contact neighbours together are also capped, M12a); a carried bundle is never charged to a contact's sender allowance. Carriers store only class 0 to 3 bundles (never bulk); evict by the bundle's creation hour (global, not the local receive time) then bundle ID, so carriers converge (Knit); keep a seen window of expiry + 3 hours (Kabootar); typed refusal reasons (quota, tag quota, neighbour quota, expired, bulk, version); quotas rebuilt by scanning at start; receipts return as ordinary class 0 bundles, batched, with a random 0 to 60 second delay on the carried path. *Copy tokens (finding 16).* Binary spray-and-wait, 6 copies for classes 0 to 2 and 2 for class 3. A hand-over is a two-step journalled transaction: the giver records "handing n tokens", the taker answers `ACCEPT` with the count it stored, the giver commits; a crash between leaves the giver holding the tokens (a duplicate budget is possible, a lost one is not). The last token goes only to a phone that asks for the bundle's tag, which a carrier cannot verify, so the copy limits are cooperative and are not claimed to hold against a malicious carrier. *Digest (finding 15).* `HELLO` carries the first 8 bytes of SHA-256 over the sorted list of (bundle ID, chunks-held bitmap hash) of everything held; equal digests may skip the SUMMARY but never replace it: a full SUMMARY is exchanged at least every 6 hours per neighbour regardless. XOR of IDs is not used (manipulable, blind to partial chunks). *Per-message tags (finding 17, Experimental, blocking before it is promoted).* Directional chains: `Kdir = HKDF(Kp, "tag-dir" || sender node ID || recipient node ID)`, `tag n = HMAC(Kdir, counter n)[0:8]`, the sender persists its counter, the receiver precomputes the next 32 and keeps skipped tags for up to 256; on every direct meeting the `HELLO` carries each side's next counter so a stranded window re-aligns over the authenticated link, and the daily tag stays valid in parallel. Opt-in "Help carry messages" (default off, 64 MB, 50 MB/day out, paused below 20% battery), a visible "Carrying n messages" and "Clear carried messages".

**M11. Metadata exposure.** Phase 1 puts nothing on air that the link does not already protect: an eavesdropper sees one call-transport link; the adjacent contact learns what it is sent. For carriers (phase 3) the earlier table overstated the hiding of the recipient (finding 6); the corrected table:

| Observer | Learns | Does not learn |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Radio eavesdropper | Link presence, sizes and timing of records | Anything inside BMS1 |
| Adjacent phone that is a contact | The conversation, because it is the endpoint | n/a |
| Carrier | Which adjacent phones (by link identity, which rotates with the discovery epoch when rotating IDs are on) handed it each bundle and took it; created and expiry hour; class 0 to 3; padded size band; a pairwise daily tag (unlinkable across days and pairs, but shared by every bundle the pair creates that UTC day, and constant for one bundle's life); the copy count it was given | The sender's or recipient's node identity from the bundle itself, the content, attachments (bulk is never carried) |
| Carrier that hands a bundle to its recipient | That the phone it is talking to is the recipient of tag T (the recipient asks for the tag, takes the bundle and sends `DELIVERED_LOCAL`), so it learns the recipient's link identity for that epoch, and with the neighbour that gave it the bundle, a candidate pair | The content; which node ID the link identity is, when rotating IDs are on and the carrier is not a contact |
| Colluding carriers | The path of a bundle, the daily count per pair, and the recipient link identities they each served | Content. Inherent to delay-tolerant networking; stated in the user text. Decoy requests and delayed `DELIVERED_LOCAL` reduce but do not remove it |
| Carrier, prekey and ratchet fields | A masked prekey ID (bootstrap) or an encrypted ratchet header (sessions): neither the kind of key, a serial number nor a ratchet public key | Which key or which message number |
| Anyone who later obtains the static keys `R` and `S` and holds captured bundles | The pair and the day of each captured bundle (the tag derives from `Kp`) | The payload and the sender field, if the prekey or message key was deleted (the metadata gap of finding 5) |
| Seized sender or recipient phone | Everything in the archive that has not been deleted; with phase 2, nothing more about bundles whose key was deleted that was not already opened | n/a |

**M12. Abuse and bounds.** All numbers are hop-local and per authenticated adjacent identity unless M12a says global. Reassembly is keyed by (peer, bundle ID) and bounded by the header's chunk count (at most 256), each chunk is checked against the hash list before it is stored (poisoned chunks counted, four abandon the session), a segment ID not named by an accepted manifest is never fetched, manifests are bounded (name 96 B, MIME 100 B, thumbnail 16 KiB, 62 segment entries), decoders run only after a tap and on size-bounded input (header check at 40 megapixels but decoded down-sampled to display size, thumbnails JPEG only and at most 320 px), and the reader of any message runs inside a catch that turns every failure into a counted refusal. Per contact: 100 waiting messages, 200 messages and 1,000 receipts per day, 512 MiB per day, 25% of the vault; per exchange 128 MiB; unknown adjacent phones keep the v1 numbers. Proof of work stays an optional phase 3 measure for non-contact senders.

**M12a. Physical accounting, global caps and control budgets (findings 12 and 19).**
- *Reserve before accepting.* Before a `WANT` is sent or a `HEADER` accepted, the receiver reserves the physical bytes the transfer can occupy: staged ciphertext of the bundle (at most 2.1 MB per segment), the final vault bytes, the metadata rows and the tombstone. Staged segments are imported into the vault and deleted one at a time, so the peak for a file is vault size + two segments. Reservations are summed over every transfer in flight; a transfer that does not fit is refused `quota`. The free-space floor is 200 MB of the device's storage: below it, nothing new is accepted.
- *Global ingress.* Besides the per-neighbour limits, all non-contact neighbours together may supply at most 64 MB per day and 16 MB per hour, all contacts together at most 1 GiB per day; at most 8 bundles are in flight (receiving) at once; metadata is bounded (20,000 seen/tombstone entries, 2,000 bundle records, 2,000 archive rows), so rotating link identities (identity churn) cannot multiply the allowance.
- *Liveness is not progress.* A link has two independent deadlines: connection liveness (any valid record, a PING counts; 60 seconds in a chat session) and transfer progress (a transfer that has a running job must move a chunk every 10 seconds, whatever pings arrive; 120 seconds total per non-chat exchange). A chat session that is idle but live ends after 30 minutes or 2 minutes without the thread open. A PING more often than once per 10 seconds is a protocol violation.
- *Control budgets.* At most 20 control records per second and 2,000 per 10 minutes per link (SUMMARY, WANT, CANCEL, PING, receipts in flight); at most 60 `CANCEL` per minute and 30 `WANT` for the same bundle per hour; at most 30 asymmetric operations (slot opens, signature checks) per exchange from an unauthenticated or non-contact peer. Exceeding a budget ends the exchange with `DONE(rate limited)`.

**M13. Groups (phase 4, sketch only).** Not in this design and not wired: until groups are designed, bundle headers of versions 3, 4 and 5 are required to carry **exactly one** key slot, and a decoder rejects any other slot count (finding 8: a slot authenticates the fixed header fields and the hash list but not the other slots, while the bundle ID hashes every slot, so extra slots could be changed to mint a new ID for a message the recipient can still open and bypass ID-only deduplication). A group design must therefore add a signed *logical message ID* and a commitment to the complete recipient set (a hash of all slot tags in every slot's AAD), a creator-signed roster with an epoch and a takeover check (bitchat `GroupProtocol.swift:63-71`), a re-key on every roster change delivered as a bundle (bitchat's weakness is that offline members miss it), the sender authenticated inside each slot as today, and per-member read receipts shown as counts. Group calls and group chats share the roster; design it once with Design B. Not scheduled.

#### Where the conversations live in the app (open question Q-M1)

The approved tabs are Live, Calls, Contacts and Settings (UI spec of 2026-10-12: nobody appears twice on one screen; Contacts = saved people only; Calls = Nearby | Recent). Because messages go only to contacts, every conversation is a contact, which makes the options small:

| Option | What it is | Tradeoffs |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **B. Contacts rows become conversation rows (recommended for phase 1)** | No new tab. A contact row keeps its name, verified check and the voice/video buttons, and gains a one-line preview (or "Nearby"), a time, an unread count and the delivery glyph of the last own message; rows with activity sort first, then A to Z. Tap opens the thread (call buttons in its top bar). Add contact stays pinned. The Messages entry in Advanced goes away when the switch is promoted | Cheapest and consistent with "nobody twice": one place per person. The Contacts tab becomes both address book and inbox, so a person with no messages sits among threads; no list of conversations that survive deleting a contact |
| A. A fifth tab "Chats" | Standard conversation list beside Contacts; five tabs are allowed by Material navigation guidance | Costs one tab for a beta feature, repeats every contact name on a second tab, and reopens the approved four-tab decision. It is the right shape if messaging becomes a main use; the data model (thread = query over contacts and messages) is the same, so B to A is a UI move |
| C. Merge into Calls (the tab becomes "Chats", with a segmented control Chats, Nearby, Calls) | WhatsApp-like single place for talking to people | Overloads the tab: the Nearby list exists to place calls and scans while visible; three segments hide the primary action |
| D. A header icon with a count on Contacts that opens an inbox screen | Smallest UI change | A third screen listing the same people; the thread still needs its own page |

The notification opens the thread directly in every option. Whatever the answer, the thread screen is the same:

- *Top bar:* back, name with the verified check, "Nearby" or "Not nearby" as plain text, voice and video buttons, an info sheet (safety number, disappearing timer, read receipts for this contact, "Block", "Delete conversation").
- *Messages:* plain rows on black with sent bubbles in the raised surface and received in the plain surface, no coloured bubbles (the red is reserved for live and hang up; failure uses an icon and the words "Not sent" with Retry). Each bubble shows the time and the state glyph (clock, one check, two checks, two filled checks); a tap on the glyph shows its words ("Delivered to Rahul's phone, 14:00"). Date separators; unread marker line.
- *File card:* type icon (a picture thumbnail only when automatic previews are on), name (middle ellipsis), size, state: *Download 24 MB*, a progress ring with percent and time left from the measured rate, *Open* and *Save*, *Waiting for Rahul to be nearby*, *Damaged, ask again*. Cancel while sending or receiving.
- *Composer:* a 56 dp text field, an attach button that opens a bottom sheet (Photo or video, File, Voice note), and a microphone that becomes Send when there is text. A voice note records while held (slide to cancel) with a visible time limit. Draft text is kept per thread. Everything the screen shows is `FLAG_SECURE` as today.
- *Copy rules (UI spec):* sentence case, no raw IDs, explanations behind an (i) sheet, settings names in the words big apps use ("Read receipts", "Download files automatically", "Disappearing messages").

#### Throughput and the sizes that make sense

Goodput here is link payload including a few percent of framing (hash lists, tags, 8 KiB chunk records); connection setup adds 2 to 6 s once per session (not per message in a chat session). Planning rates are the measured ones: healthy 300 to 1000 kbps, the weak ASUS to A15s GATT pair about 65 kbps. A walk-past contact (28 s) leaves 0.7 to 1.5 MB.

| Item | Bytes on the link | 1000 kbps | 300 kbps | 65 kbps |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Text, receipt | about 1.3 KB | under 1 s | under 1 s | under 1 s |
| Photo, default (1600 px) | 300 KB | 2.5 s | 8 s | 38 s |
| Voice note, 2 min | 360 KB | 3 s | 10 s | 46 s |
| Inline limit | 1 MB | 8 s | 28 s | 2.1 min |
| File | 10 MB | 82 s | 4.6 min | 21 min |
| Largest file | 64 MB | 8.8 min | 29 min | 2.3 h |

So: text, photos and voice notes are comfortable everywhere; a file of 1 MB can complete in one walk-past; 10 MB is a sit-together transfer (and a weak pair needs a coffee); 64 MB is practical only on a healthy link and with a Wi-Fi bearer would be minutes. The send sheet shows the estimate from the learned rate for that contact, and a transfer that is slower than its estimate says so.

#### Switches (register in [SWITCHES.md](SWITCHES.md); all off or the control arm by default until the phone tests pass)

`test_messaging_v2` (master; Settings > Features "Messages v2" under Messages; needs Messages; default off, constant `MessagingV2Defaults.ENABLED`). Sub-switches: `test_mv2_chat_session` (the thread link; `false` is the control arm, exchange per message as v1), `test_mv2_bulk_listed` (Experimental arm: list segments in `SUMMARY` and let the auto-download policy `WANT` them, against the receiver-pull default), `test_mv2_read_receipts` (default off, also a user setting), `test_mv2_autodownload_mb` (8; 0 never, 64 all), `test_mv2_max_file_mb` (64), `test_mv2_photo_edge` and `test_mv2_photo_quality` (1600, 80), `test_mv2_chats_tab` (Experimental arm for option A of Q-M1: a fifth tab), `test_mv2_show_previews` (message text in the conversation list, default on), `test_mv2_prekeys` (phase 2; off = static-key slots, marked in the log as not forward secret and refused by any contact whose minimum-version pin is 4 or higher), `test_mv2_ratchet` (phase 2b; on = Double Ratchet sessions, off = a prekey seal per message, option A; both arms kept), `test_mv2_auto_thumbnails` (off; automatic thumbnail decoding, allowed only after isolated decoding is proven on the whole reference set; otherwise a generic icon is shown until the user taps), `test_mv2_message_tags` (phase 3, per-message tags), `test_mv2_background_check` (phase 3, periodic duty-cycled check for messages, off). With `test_messaging_v2` off every byte on air and on the link is exactly v1's (bundle version 2, `BSM1`, `HELLO` version 1).

#### Phases, scope and size estimates (not a commitment)

Sizes are Kotlin and XML lines changed or added, then JVM test lines, then agent-days of focused work including JVM tests; phone time is separate and needs the phones free.

| Phase | Scope | Main | Tests | Agent-days | Phone days | Done when |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | --- |
| 0 Measure v1 first | With the v1 code and its DEBUG controls (`sf_send`, `sf_sync`): store-and-forward goodput over GATT, CoC and Classic on the required phones (the 300-1000 and 65 kbps planning rates are call-stream rates, not measured here), setup time, a `BENCHMARK message_transfer` line, and background reception (T-M8). Decides the time table, the 1 MB inline limit, the chunk size and whether a background check is needed | 0.2 k | 0.1 k | 1 | 2 | Numbers recorded in the time table; v1 itself gets its first phone pass |
| 1a Core | `BSM2`, `ATTACHMENT`/`SEGMENT`, bundle version 3 with class 4 (tag-only header), unlisted `WANT`, `CANCEL`, priority writer, file vault, receipts with status and batching, admission tiers, retention split, per-contact caps, error containment, `BENCHMARK message_transfer`, the message ID and (sender, message ID) dedup, exactly-one-slot decoding, immutable sealed bytes, physical reservations with global caps and control budgets (second review: findings 3, 8, 11, 12, 14, 18, 19), no automatic decoding | 1.8 k | 1.2 k | 5-6 | | JVM vectors in PROTOCOL pass (parser rejects, tamper, wrong recipient, chunk poison, resume at every chunk boundary, truncated vault, segment not in manifest, hostile names and MIME) |
| 1b Attachment pipeline | Photo/video picker (Android Photo Picker, falls back to `GetContent`), file picker (`OpenDocument`), re-encode with orientation and alpha handling, JPEG segment filter, lossless video remux, JPEG thumbnail, magic-byte sniffing, isolated-process decoder spike, voice reuse, safe Open provider (`openProxyFileDescriptor`, one-time grants), Save | 1.3 k | 0.6 k | 4-5 | | Decoders bounded; no plaintext file in cache after Open (checked by listing cache and files directories) |
| 1d Link and contacts | Chat session and the link state-machine changes listed under M6 (repeated `SUMMARY`, held `DONE`, re-`WANT`, tag-table refresh, 60 s watchdog, cooldown after `HELLO`, tie-break), card auto-accept and freshness rule, key-changed state, migration of v1 cards and queued v1 messages | 1.3 k | 0.8 k | 5-6 | | Session ends within one chunk of a call; cooldown rules unit-tested |
| **Phase 1 total** | any file, chat UX, receipts, adjacent contacts only, no carriers | **about 6.0 k** | **about 3.0 k** | **20-23** | **8-10** | Tests T-M1 to T-M9, T-M15, T-M16 and T-M17 pass on the reference set |
| 2 Forward secrecy: prekey bootstrap | Blocking first: the Keystore alias spike (creation time and limits for 200 aliases on the required phones). Then prekey batches and the signed `BPK1`, slot version 4 with masked prekey IDs, file-key derivation for segments, the minimum-version pin, spent-ID tracking, the consumption journal, budgets and caps, per-prekey Keystore wrapping, `use-until` lifetimes, the message-ID re-seal rule, user text. With `test_mv2_ratchet` off this is option A. Depends on 1d (mid-exchange SUMMARY) | 1.6 k | 1.0 k | 7-8 | 2 | T-M10, T-M11 and T-M16 pass |
| 2b Forward secrecy: sessions | X3DH-style bootstrap into Double Ratchet sessions, slot version 5 with encrypted headers, skipped keys purged at expiry, simultaneous start, session reset, the shared journal; byte layouts and vectors first (blocking) | 1.8 k | 1.8 k | 8-10 | 2 | T-M18 passes; `test_mv2_ratchet` becomes the default |
| 3 Carriers | Opt-in carrier role on, two admission paths, version-4/5-only admission, quotas, convergent eviction, journalled copy tokens, digest with periodic full SUMMARY, receipts over carriers, "Handed on" state, optional per-message tag chains and background check, battery budget; blocked until findings 1, 5, 6, 7, 15, 16 and 17 are closed in code and Q10 and Q-M11 are answered | 2.0 k | 1.0 k | 8-10 | 4-5 | T-M12 and T-M13 pass; Q10 answered |
| 4 Groups | Roster, epochs, multi-slot bundles, receipts as counts | about 2.5 k | about 1.5 k | later | later | Own design record and review |

Order: phase 0 first (it can run now on the v1 build), then 1a, then 1b and 1d in parallel (disjoint files), then 1c.


**Notifications (built 2026-10-13, branch `feature/mv2-notifications`, JVM tests only).** One notification per conversation under one generic group summary, channel "Messages" (default importance, set by the user in system settings; the v1 quiet channel is removed while Messaging v2 is on). Every notification is `VISIBILITY_PRIVATE`: a locked phone shows the app name and "New message" (or "N new messages"), and the contact's label only when "Show names on lock screen" is on, never text. The unlocked version shows the label by the naming precedence of the Contacts tab and the text of a single unread text message; a file shows only its kind ("Photo", "File"); a message with a disappearing timer, several unread messages, a message from an older app, or a contact without a safe label show no text, so a notification never outlives a message meant to vanish and never prints a raw identifier. "Mark as read" is offered only when the notification showed the whole message (a single unread text), so a read receipt cannot claim an unseen message; the engine still sends a receipt only when both phones turned receipts on. No notification while that thread is open and the app is in front; the notification is cancelled when the thread opens, the messages are read, deleted or the conversation is deleted, the contact is removed, or on wipe. A tap opens the Contacts tab with that thread open through the conversation ID (a hash of both node IDs, never a node ID), checked against the accepted contacts on open; a live call, stream or camera screen is not switched away from. The permission is asked once, when the first conversation opens (Android 13+); a refusal is respected. Nothing is posted by a process that was restarted without the switch on. One reading choice: "FLAG_SECURE-sensitive" is applied as "a message meant not to persist (a disappearing timer), or whose text is not fully shown, never gives text to the notification shade".

#### Phone tests and gates

Gates are the same for every phone; eligibility follows actual capabilities (transport, Android version, storage). `MessagesActivity` is `FLAG_SECURE`, so a screenshot is black: DEBUG builds started by a benchmark launch skip the flag for the owned test phones, and every state is also checked from an accessibility dump. Matrices are covered pairwise (every phone as sender and as receiver on every transport it supports, every size and format at least once per transport), not as a full product. Scope: the six required phones (S24, S22, M31, ASUS, Xiaomi, A55) with the A15s as the legacy-stack comparator. Pairs are chosen so every phone is sender and receiver, over GATT, LE CoC (where supported) and Classic. Cases are generated through the suite inventory when implemented (new case IDs, input type "generated file", exclusive phone aliases); no pass is inherited from the v1 messages tests. Throughput and timing are measured from the app's own `BENCHMARK message_transfer` lines (bytes, chunk count, setup time, first and last chunk times, resume count); screen state is checked by screenshot of the thread (not the S24) and by accessibility dump. Quality of re-encoded pictures is a separate, UNMEASURED dimension until a picture-quality adapter exists.

| Test | Setup | Pass when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| T-M1 content matrix | Generated files: 1 KB text, 300 KB JPEG, 1,000,000 B (the inline limit) and 1,000,001 B (smallest segmented), 10 MB, 64,000,000 B; formats JPEG, PNG, PDF, ZIP, MP4, TXT, an APK, a file with no extension; every pair direction, 3 transports | SHA-256 identical end to end; goodput and time to Delivered recorded and within 1.5 times the estimate from the learned rate; inline sizes need one bundle, larger ones the manifest plus segments; no corrupt file |
| T-M3 call preemption and A/B | 10 MB transfer while a call starts, then a call running while a transfer is requested; calls with and without a background transfer on the same pair | The transfer stops within one chunk (at most 1.1 s at 65 kbps); the call's existing gates are unchanged against the no-transfer arm (the preservation comparison); the transfer resumes after hang-up. Also: a call started from an open thread with a chat session running (session on against off) has the same call-setup time as the control arm |
| T-M4 weak pair | ASUS to A15s over GATT at 65 kbps: 300 KB photo, 2 minute voice note, 1 MB file; then a 5 MB file | Photo and voice note complete without session timeouts; progress and estimate shown; the 5 MB file completes across sessions or is reported as measured, not hidden |
| T-M5 receipts and states | Delivered, Read with receipts on at both, on at one, off at both; contact moves out of range; receipt over the chat session; failed attempts to "Not sent" | Ticks match the state table; no read receipt when either side is off; no receipt for a receipt; a file shows Delivered when the card arrived and Downloaded only when every segment is verified; screenshots (not S24) and accessibility text agree |
| T-M6 receiver control | Manifest only; auto-download at 0, 8 and 64 MB; tap Download, cancel at 40 percent, tap again; sender deletes the message mid-download; vault full | Segments are fetched only when approved; cancel (`CANCEL`) releases airtime within one chunk and a second Download in the same session works; delete withdraws the file and the receiver shows "No longer available"; a full vault refuses with a visible reason and loses nothing else |
| T-M7 chat session | Open thread on both phones, 100 short texts at 1 per 2 s; 30 minutes idle with the thread open; background and foreground changes; battery from batterystats | Text p50/p95 latency recorded (target p95 under 3 s on a healthy link); no session without a ping for 60 s; battery cost per hour reported against the control arm, not scored; session closes within one chunk of a call |
| T-M8 lifecycle (first run in phase 0 on the v1 build) | Screen off and Doze, app in background, process killed, Bluetooth toggled, airplane mode, on each Android release and OEM skin | Messages are delivered when the app is in the foreground; whether and when they arrive in the background is recorded as measured (this decides `test_mv2_background_check`); nothing is lost or duplicated; no crash |
| T-M9 hostile input (JVM plus phone) | File names with `../`, RTL override, 10,000 characters, control characters; MIME that lies; 60 megapixel PNG; truncated JPEG; a manifest naming 64 segments that do not exist; a segment with a wrong hash; a bundle with an unknown kind or version; the 0.2.0-beta APK as peer | All refused or contained as specified, no crash, no ANR, memory under the declared ceiling, no plaintext file left behind, no picture decoded before a tap (unless `test_mv2_auto_thumbnails` is on, and that switch is allowed only with isolated decoding proven here); the older build ends the exchange cleanly and the thread shows nothing half-delivered |
| T-M10 forward secrecy (phase 2) | 20 messages against a 20-key batch and one more; reorder and duplicate; delete a prekey and replay the bundle; backup-less reinstall; clock skew of 2 h; a replayed signed batch after keys were consumed; a crash injected between each step of the consumption journal | Every message opens once; the 21st uses the rotating key; a replay after deletion is refused (`PREKEY_GONE` or claimed key) and a re-seal under the same message ID never produces a second bubble; a replayed batch resurrects nothing; after every injected crash the message is stored exactly once and its one-time key is gone before the delivery is acknowledged (host test of the key files and Keystore aliases); refill arrives in a live exchange |
| T-M11 prekey abuse (JVM) | Exhaustion by a contact, forged batch (wrong signer, wrong audience), stale generation, batch for another contact, a peer that always reports `prekeys held` 0, 1,000 slots that fail to open, 400 outstanding keys, Keystore alias cap | Only the audited contact's batch is accepted; exhaustion affects only that contact; issued keys never exceed 40 per contact, 400 overall, 200 aliases; refills at most 2 per hour per contact and 12 overall; failed opens charge double and end the exchange after 20 per hour |
| T-M12 carriers (phase 3) | A to B to C with B unable to open the content; 4 to 6 phones in two groups plus a walking carrier | Delivery ratio, latency, duplicate bytes, per-phone airtime and overnight battery measured; bulk never carried; no live-session regression |
| T-M13 carrier abuse | Quota fill by a stranger, eviction order, tag quota, rate limits, clock skew | Convergent eviction (two carriers hold the same set after sync); limits as specified |
| T-M15 additional risks | Two contacts sending at once (fairness and per-contact isolation); disk full during a segment write; a received JPEG and MP4 contain no location or device metadata; the lock-screen notification shows no text or sender; a real external viewer opens a file (grant, revoke after leaving the thread, descriptor limit); upgrade from v1 data (cards, queued messages); a wrong sender clock (hour 0, one day ahead) places no message out of its window; a disappearing timer below the floor | As specified; nothing lost, no crash |
| T-M14 preservation | Calls, streams, relayed calls and discovery on the changed stages | Existing gates unchanged: one call pair and one stream chain per transport with the feature on and an idle thread, and with it off |
| T-M16 identity and downgrade (JVM plus phone) | A bundle with two slots, a slot altered in a second-slot header, a replayed message with a new bundle ID and the same message ID, a version 3 bundle after the contact is pinned at 4, a version 2 or 3 bundle offered to a carrier | Slot counts other than 1 are rejected; the duplicate message is dropped; the downgrade is refused and shown; legacy versions are never carried or re-offered |
| T-M17 resource accounting (JVM plus phone) | Three simultaneous 60 MB downloads from three contacts against a small vault and a nearly full device; 100 rotating link identities each offering the non-contact allowance; PING flooding every 1 s; CANCEL and WANT loops; 10,000 seen entries | Reservations refuse what does not fit, peak disk is vault + two segments per transfer, the free-space floor holds, the global ingress caps hold under identity churn, a PING never keeps a stalled transfer alive past 10 s, control budgets end abusive exchanges |
| T-M18 sessions (phase 2b; JVM plus phone) | Reference-script vectors; 300 messages with 30 percent loss and shuffled order; skip beyond 256; crash at each step of the commit; simultaneous start; state corruption and reset; a message older than its purge time | Every deliverable message opens once in any order within the window; nothing outside it opens; keys are gone after use; a reset is visible and re-seals queued messages under the same message IDs; encrypted headers hide the ratchet key from a carrier (capture check) |

**Independent review (2026-10-12, fresh-context reviewer against the source and the three documents).** No finding invalidated the approach; the review confirmed the size arithmetic (a full segment is 1,044,572 plaintext bytes in 128 chunks (1,044,588 after the message ID was added), 255 blocks, 62 segments for 64,000,000 bytes, slot sizes 154 and 158, the header with 8 slots fits 8 KiB), the time table, that the unlisted-`WANT` rule opens no oracle, that a v1 peer fails closed on a v2 `HELLO`, and that the v1 figures quoted here match the code. Accepted and folded into the text above: M-R1 (the prekey ID was visible to carriers and linked a pair's bundles: now masked with the pair secret), M-R2 (one prekey per segment would exhaust a batch and push files to the rotating key: segments derive their key from a file key in the manifest and carry no slot), M-R3 (key lifetimes did not cover bundle lifetimes: `use-until` plus 15 days), M-R4 (refill and outstanding-key caps, lookup by (contact, ID), immediate batch after `PREKEY_GONE`), M-R5 (precise statement of what forward secrecy achieves), M-R6 (per-prekey Keystore keys on API 31+, 5-key sub-batches below, best-effort erasure stated), M-R7 (conversation ID bound to the authenticated sender), M-R8 (the link state-machine changes are listed and the estimates raised), M-R9 (`CANCEL`, re-`WANT`, chunk counts in the manifest, synthesized admission entry), M-R10 (Delivered versus Downloaded, status 3), M-R11 (inline bytes in the vault, 25% per contact, consistent queue numbers), M-R12 (receipts have their own allowance), M-R13 (sniffed type overrides the claim, allow-list for Open, not-exported provider with revocable grants, copying by the other app stated), M-R14 (JPEG-only thumbnails, down-sampled decode, isolated process), M-R15 (video remux, JPEG segment filter, orientation and alpha), M-R16 (call from an open thread added to T-M3 and T-M14), M-R17 (phase 0 measures v1 first), M-R18 (phone days raised to 8-10, pairwise matrices, `FLAG_SECURE` handling for tests, the missing benchmark line, T-M15), M-R19 to M-R25 (clock window, text cap 8000, vault IDs, immediate receipts in a session, tombstone check, card freshness and migration, timer floor, partial-download exemption, previews setting). The first round did not adopt decoding thumbnails only in an isolated process (it kept a bounded in-process fallback); the second review (finding 10) reversed that: nothing is decoded automatically. M-R6's 5-key sub-batches were likewise replaced by per-prekey wrapping (finding 13).


| # | Sev | Finding | Verdict | Resolution |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | High | Phase 2 still accepts static-key versions; FS is not enforced for carriers | Adopted (phase 2/3) | Minimum security version pinned per contact, versions 2 and 3 never carried, carrier admission accepts 4 and 5 only; phase 3 blocked until coded (M9, PROTOCOL Versions) |
| 2 | High | Equal-generation batches could resurrect spent keys | Adopted (phase 2) | Serial IDs never reused, spent-ID ranges persisted before use, ID/key rebinding refused, equal generation idempotent |
| 3 | High | Re-seal changes the dedup identity; a carrier refusal must not authorise it | Adopted (message ID in phase 1, rest phase 2) | Random message ID in `BSM2`, dedup on (sender, message ID); re-seal only on a refusal from the authenticated adjacent recipient or an end-to-end receipt status 4; T-M10 fixed |
| 4 | High | Open, tombstone and key deletion are separate persistence systems | Adopted (phase 2) | One journalled consumption transaction, a one-time key claimed by its first bundle, acknowledgement after commit |
| 5 | High | The tag derives from static `Kp`: relationships leak after a later key compromise | Partly | Payload and sender-field secrecy are claimed; addressing metadata forward secrecy is not, and the text now says so (M9, M11, PROTOCOL); erasable tag chains are the Experimental route; Q-M11 asks the user; phase 3 blocked until answered |
| 6 | High | Carriers can identify the recipient (link identity, WANT, `DELIVERED_LOCAL`) | Partly | M11 corrected and the recipient-serving carrier row added; rotating IDs, decoy requests and delayed `DELIVERED_LOCAL` reduce it; it is inherent to delivering to a phone and is disclosed, not removed |
| 7 | High | v1's recipient-slot admission cannot work for an opaque carrier | Adopted (phase 3) | Two admission paths specified (M10, PROTOCOL carriers) |
| 8 | High | A slot does not authenticate the other slots; the ID hashes all of them | Adopted (phase 1) | Exactly one slot in versions 3 to 5; groups need a signed logical ID and a recipient-set commitment (M13) |
| 9 | High | Forty keys per contact do not bound work; exceptional refills unbudgeted | Adopted (phase 2) | 400 overall, 12 batches per hour overall, `PREKEY_GONE` refills inside the limits, failed opens charged double |
| 10 | High | A JPEG thumbnail is a zero-click native parser; heap limits are not isolation | Adopted (phase 1) | Nothing is decoded automatically; generic icon until a tap; automatic previews only after isolated decoding is proven on every reference phone (`test_mv2_auto_thumbnails`, Q-M12) |
| 11 | High | Index-derived GCM nonces need immutable plaintext; retry/repair not forbidden | Adopted (phase 1) | Sealed bytes immutable; vault blocks written once or with identical bytes; changes get fresh keys (M7, PROTOCOL vault) |
| 12 | High | Logical quotas omit physical accounting; identity churn | Adopted (phase 1) | M12a: reservations including staging, a free-space floor, global ingress caps, metadata row caps |
| 13 | Medium | API 29-30 sub-batch rewrites leave older copies decryptable | Adopted (phase 2) | One wrapping key per prekey on every API level, 200-alias cap, measured in a blocking spike |
| 14 | Medium | The archived manifest keeps the file key | Adopted (phase 1) | File key in a separate transfer record, erased at completion, abandonment or expiry |
| 15 | Medium | XOR digest is manipulable and blind to partial chunks | Adopted (phase 3) | SHA-256 over the sorted (ID, completion hash) list, an optimisation only, full SUMMARY at least every 6 hours |
| 16 | Medium | Copy tokens have no durable commit; no destination proof | Partly (phase 3) | Journalled two-step hand-over; limits described as cooperative because a carrier cannot verify the recipient |
| 17 | Medium | A 32-counter tag window strands messages; shared `Kp` collides | Adopted (phase 3, still Experimental) | Directional chains, persisted counters, 256 skipped, resynchronisation in `HELLO`; blocking before promotion |
| 18 | Medium | Ignoring already-receipted IDs drops later read/downloaded receipts | Adopted (phase 1) | Independent monotonic flags, dedup by (bundle, status) |
| 19 | Medium | Any record resets the watchdog; no control budgets | Adopted (phase 1) | Liveness and progress deadlines separated, PING rate limited, control and asymmetric-operation budgets (M12a) |
| 20 | Medium | The Double Ratchet was rejected on a wrong premise | Adopted | M9 redone; the recommendation changes from A to B (bootstrap plus Double Ratchet), built in phases 2 and 2b, A kept as the arm `test_mv2_ratchet` off |

**Promotion criteria.** The master switch stays off until T-M1 to T-M9, T-M14, T-M15, T-M16 and T-M17 pass on the whole reference set and the user has answered Q-M1 to Q-M4. The prekey and carrier switches follow their own tests and Q10. The Experimental arms (bulk listed, fifth tab, message tags, background check) stay switchable and off; each is promoted only by its own A/B. Blocking before phase 2: the Keystore alias spike and the answer to Q-M5. Blocking before phase 2b: byte layouts and vectors for the sessions. Blocking before phase 3: findings 1, 5, 6, 7, 15, 16 and 17 coded and tested, and Q10 and Q-M11 answered.

#### Dial failures, cooldown after a lost link, progress-aware total, and the aligned-writes A/B (2026-10-13)

**1. The A15s dial to the ASUS failed 6 of 7 times.** Symptom: the A15s (OPPO, Android 10, GATT client) connects, subscribes, requests 2M and the exchange never gets the peer's link hello; about 5 s later it reports `EOFException`, and the ASUS (GATT server) reports `incoming_handshake: result=failed | reason=EOFException` a further 5 s later. In the type-b failures the link hello completed and the exchange then crawled or died within 15 s (`helloMs` 1.0 to 2.5 s instead of 0.3 s, `hello=-1` and `INTERRUPTED` or `TIMEOUT` at 5 to 13 s). The ASUS-initiated exchanges (ASUS central) were 100% good in the same runs. Cause: the GATT server read path is not at fault (the CCCD write, MTU 517 on both sides and the first writes are ordered correctly: Android delivers the stack's one-way server callbacks to the app one at a time and in order, and the pipe exists before the first write is delivered). The failure is the one already measured for calls with HCI snoop (`AdaptivePhyMemory.SETUP_STALL_MS`: with the A15s central to the ASUS, the ASUS to A15s direction stops right after LE PHY Update Complete on 2M; the A15s times out 5 s later and the ASUS 5 s after that). The log timing matches exactly: every ASUS-side EOF came 4.9 to 5.0 s after the A15s side, and the A15s side came 5.0 s after the PHY update. The call dial has had the 2M protections since 2026-10-10 (stall guard, one 1M retry to the callee's current advertisement, bad-2M memory); the message-exchange dial (`startMessageSync`) opened its link with the endpoint PHY and none of them. Fix: `MessageDialPhy` gives the message dial the same three protections plus an early-loss rule (an authenticated 2M exchange that died within 15 s with no HELLO and no chunk marks the peer bad on 2M). Switch `test_message_adaptive_phy` (default on; SWITCHES). Re-check: A15s dials the ASUS 10 times, expect `adaptive_phy: link=message ... setup_stall` then `link_phy ... effective=1M` on the first, 1M from the second on, all complete; and the preservation arms (ASUS dials the A15s, S24 and M31 pairs unchanged on 2M).

**2. The proto=1 attempt before the proto=2 session is not a second dial.** The app starts one link per `sf_sync` control or tap. `proto=1` on a session line only means "the peer's HELLO was not read": a responder that refuses with `DONE(rate limited)` answers before its own HELLO, and a link lost before the HELLO never learns the version. No peer was marked legacy in these runs (no `does not speak Messaging v2`, `PEER_APP_OLDER` or `outcome=FAILED`). What did produce extra attempts: (a) each admitted exchange that the link cut before anything moved still stamped the responder's 60 s per-neighbour cooldown, so the retry 46 s after a failed A15s dial was refused `RATE_LIMITED` (10:38:35 interrupted, 10:39:21 refused); (b) the driver's own `sf_sync` controls arrived inside the cooldown of the previous finished exchange (for example 16 s and 22 s after a completed one), which the cooldown correctly refuses. Fix for (a): the responder gives the 60 s spacing back when an admitted exchange ends `INTERRUPTED` or `TIMEOUT` with no chunk moved and nothing opened (`NeighborLedger.refundCooldown`); the hourly count is not refunded, so connect-and-vanish is still bounded to 10 an hour (60 for a chat contact). (b) is the cooldown working; a driver that wants a second exchange waits 65 s (the auto-sync interval already does). Not changed: a v2 pair that both offer `CHAT` is already admitted after 5 s.

**3. The 120 s total limit cut slow transfers.** The A15s answering (GATT server, notifications) moved 164 chunks (1,337,296 bytes) in 120 s at 89 kbps: the first 1 MB segment (125 chunks, 1,017,808 bytes) finished at about 91 s and the next queued bundle was cut after 39 chunks, so the session ended `TIMEOUT`, the cut bundle waited for another session behind the cooldown, and a 5 MB file (about 1.05 MB per segment, 4 segments) needed about four sessions, each with a fresh link; with `test_gatt_aligned_writes` it moved 236 chunks at 128 kbps and still hit the limit (11:10:41: one segment complete at 64 s, a second cut at 54 of 128 chunks). The fixed total remains right for a quiet or stuck exchange, wrong for one that is moving. Now (non-chat, v2 only): the total is extended while the last 4 chunks (either direction) all moved within the last 10 s, up to `sessionHardCapMs` = 15 minutes (8 MB per exchange at 89 kbps is about 12 minutes); the 10 s liveness and 10 s progress deadlines are unchanged and still fire at once on a stall, and a trickle (one chunk per 9 s) passes progress but is cut at 120 s. v1 exchanges keep the fixed total. Switch `test_mv2_progress_total` (default on; off is the fixed 120 s). Re-check: a 5 MB file A15s to ASUS in one session at about 89 kbps (about 7.5 minutes) and a stalled link still ending after 10 s.

**4. The chat session outlived the opener's thread.** `ChatPresence.lastInterestMs` returned "now" whenever the app was in the foreground, on the phone that dialed too, so the dialer's `tick()` never saw its thread-left grace run out (`sf2_thread_close` on the opener, no `chat end why=thread_left` for 8+ minutes with both apps in front). Design M6 lets the foreground keep a session only on the *answering* phone. Now the tick ignores the foreground screen for a session this phone dialed (`StoreForwardLinkSession.isInitiator`); the dialer ends it with DONE(IDLE) about 2 minutes after leaving the thread, and an answering foreground phone still cannot hold it alone. Dial ownership: when both threads are open the lower node ID dials and the higher dials itself 4 s later; a phone with only its own thread open dials whatever its ID, so a failing lower-ID dial (the A15s) is already backed up by the higher-ID phone when its thread is open. No role swap was added: with item 1 fixed the failing dial is the exception, and a swap would need the other phone to hold a thread it does not have.

**A/B: `test_gatt_aligned_writes` on the A15s answering direction (candidate device-family policy, proposed, not promoted).** ASUS dials, A15s answers and sends a 1 MB file; alternating arms, three runs each, same pair, same placement, same build (f0a89e03), 120 s window (the exchange hit the total limit in all six, so goodput is read from the window):

| Arm | Run times | Chunks in 120 s | Goodput kbps (active) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| off (3 x 512-byte-value notifications = 3 LL PDUs of 251+251+17) | 11:07, 11:13, 11:18 | 164, 164, 165 | 89, 89, 89 |
| on (495-byte values = 2 LL PDUs) | 11:10, 11:16, 11:21 | 236, 236, 236 | 128, 128, 129 |

The ratio is +44% (236/164 = 1.44), reproduced exactly three times in each arm, and it is the prediction of the design: 88 to 96 kbps is one 251-octet PDU per 15 ms connection event, so the A15s controller as peripheral sends one data PDU per event and the value size decides how many events a notification costs (3 versus 2). The same phone as central sends 600 to 700 kbps with write commands (several PDUs per event), so the limit is peripheral-role scheduling, not the radio. Proposed entry for the DeviceFamilyPolicy registry (nothing is promoted: the registry needs official sources and test data beyond the two local phones): id `gatt-aligned-values-one-pdu-peripheral`, matcher = OPPO CPH2xxx / the A15s SoC and Bluetooth stack family on Android 10 AND the local role is GATT server AND `ConnectionParameters` show one PDU per event (measured, not assumed); behaviour = size notification values to whole 251-octet LE data PDUs (`gattValueBytes(mtu, alignToLePdus = true)`), off-switch `test_gatt_aligned_writes=false`; confidence = low until a second phone of the same family shows it; sources still to cite: Bluetooth Core Specification Vol 6 Part B 4.5.10 (data length extension, 251-octet data PDU payload), Vol 3 Part A 3.2.8 (L2CAP basic header) and Vol 3 Part F 3.2.9 (ATT_MTU and the 512-byte attribute limit), the AOSP GATT server notification path (`gatt_sr.cc`), and a vendor or chipset note on peripheral-role PDUs per connection event, none of which was checked in this session. Until the registry entry is reviewed, the switch stays Experimental and off by default (SWITCHES).

#### A15s as a sender: the 2M link dies on streams and calls, and the stream path had no protection (2026-10-13)

**Roles.** A stream source dials the viewer: the A15s source is the GATT client and LE central, writes ATT write commands (opcode 0x52) to the ASUS server, and the ASUS answers with notifications. A call dialed by the A15s has the same roles. The pair that works (ASUS source or caller to the A15s) has the A15s as peripheral.

**Which side closes first, and why.** The A15s link layer, by connection timeout. In all nine stream attempts that ended on their own, the A15s stack reported `onClientConnectionState status=8` (HCI reason 0x08) and the ASUS reported `Disconn Complete reason=8` 5.0 s later (the two clocks differ by 2.45 s, read from the matching PHY-update callbacks); the A15s stops transmitting when it gives up, so the peripheral's own 5 s supervision timer starts there. No local close, no status 133, 62 or 143 and no `congested` on the A15s side. `Immediate child control channel stopped; closing the link: EOFException`, `Bluetooth stopped: EOFException`, `GATT input interrupted` and the ASUS `GATT sender disconnected (end of stream)` are consequences, not causes. Time from the 2M PHY update to the A15s timeout: 7.45, 6.08, 17.65, 7.86 s (cold-1), 8.59, 10.81, 8.60 s (cold-2), 7.78, 5.38 s (control-1); the A15s last heard the ASUS 0.4 to 12.7 s after the PHY update (timeout minus 5 s), so the failure is not a fixed offset from the switch. Two more attempts ended by an app close after the link crawled (control-1: one blocking write took 8.0 s, TX 9 kbps, queue age 7.9 s).

**Which direction dies.** Peripheral to central, on 2M. The ASUS kept hearing the A15s until the A15s gave up (its timeout came exactly 5 s later), while the A15s heard nothing from the ASUS for the last 5 s. Around the switch the central to peripheral direction also stalled once: in `stream-cold-1` the 357-byte end-to-end hello write, queued on 1M just before the update, reached the ASUS 2.45 s later, after which the ASUS replied, and the A15s's timeout (timeout minus 5 s) puts its last reception at about that reply. In the call (`call-control-3`) the ASUS logged `onServerCongestion congested=true` that never cleared and an L2CAP queue of 15 packets against a quota of 20, which is a controller not getting acknowledgements. Same signature as the earlier HCI snoop (`AdaptivePhyMemory.SETUP_STALL_MS`). Logcat cannot separate the A15s receiver from the ASUS transmit path: that needs the HCI snoop (the A15s reports `mSnoopLogSettingAtEnable = full`).

**What it is not.** (1) The encoder: attempts 1, 2 and 4 of `stream-cold-1` died before any encoder was started (libaom first ran at 15:35:43), encode p95 was 48 to 64 ms (load about 0.6 to 0.7 at 320x240@10 and 640x480@14.7), and the link layer timer runs in the controller. Two attempts did die 0.4 s and 3 s after the encoder started, so a contribution on top cannot be excluded, but it is not needed. (2) Connection priority: HIGH on both, 7.5 ms then 15 ms, identical in the attempts that failed and (messages) in the 1M dials that worked. (3) Scanning: the A15s scanner (mode 2) was running in `stream-cold-1` attempt 1, but in `stream-control-1` attempt 1 it stopped (`source slots full`) 0.9 s before the last reception and the link still died. (4) Wi-Fi: both phones logged `Wi-Fi not connected`. (5) A stream-only write path: calls and streams fail the same way.

**1M works.** `lane3-asus-a15s-m31-20261013/b5-mv2-33b0b869` (message dial, same pair and roles): the first 2M link was closed at the 3 s stall guard, the 1M retry found the ASUS's new advertisement after 3.9 s and completed the exchange; the next dials started on 1M (`reason=remembered at=connect`), set up in 1.3 to 1.8 s and read the peer hello in 0.26 to 0.34 s (the `RATE_LIMITED` results are the responder's 60 s cooldown, not the link).

**Why the stream kept failing.** The call dial remembers a hop peer that went bad on 2M and starts the next link on 1M (`AdaptivePhyMemory`), the message dial got the same in `MessageDialPhy`, but the stream source had only the lookup: nothing ever recorded a bad peer for a stream, so every redial (the viewer re-requests about 8 to 13 s after each loss) used 2M again, for the whole run.

**Why the call looked different (symptom b).** `b2-weaklink-ab/call-a15s-asus-control-r1`: the stall guard fired correctly at 3.005 s and the A15s closed its link, but the ASUS (callee) kept the dead link: its disconnect callback came about 11 s later (`incoming_handshake: result=failed | reason=EOFException`, then 1.6 s to advertise again), so `rediscover=none` after 8 s, the retry connected to the old address and timed out after 12 s (`Call could not connect: Timed out connecting GATT video`). The memory was written only after a successful 1M retry, so nothing was remembered and every later call repeated this. In `call-control-3` the hop handshake took 1249 ms (inside the guard), the call went ACTIVE, and the link then died 15.7 s after the PHY update; that path already recorded the early loss.

**Changes (all with an off-switch; SWITCHES).** (1) `StreamPhyLoss` and the sender hook (`test_stream_adaptive_phy`, default on): a direct single-link GATT stream whose 2M link ended with status 8 or the sender's heartbeat timeout, with the peer's silence beginning within 15 s of hop authentication (silence start = report time minus supervision timeout, or the last heartbeat), marks the authenticated hop peer bad on 2M. A viewer leaving (19), a local close, a relay-capable peer, a fan-out source and 1M links never count. Log `BENCHMARK adaptive_phy: link=stream ... early_loss=... reason=... cause=... gattStatus=... silentAfterMs=...`. (2) `test_stall_remembers_2m` (default on): a stalled direct call setup records the advertised node bad on 2M at the stall, not only after a 1M success (not for relayed offers, rotating advertisements or other failures). (3) `test_gatt_phy_split` (Experimental, default off): `tx2m-rx1m` / `tx1m-rx2m` per-direction `setPreferredPhy`, to name the failing direction and possibly keep 2M for the A15s's media direction. JVM tests: `StreamPhyLossTest` replays the nine measured attempts (all remembered), the window arithmetic, the cases that must not record, and the masks.

**Not fixed, and why (open).** (a) The first stream or call from a new A15s-to-ASUS pair still fails: nothing predicts a pair-specific controller interaction, and a device-family entry needs sources and data beyond the two local phones (AGENTS); the registry has no entry for it. (b) With rotating discovery IDs on (the default in user builds) the viewer's advertised prefix is a pseudonym, so the pre-connect lookup cannot find a memory entry keyed by the authenticated node, for calls, messages and now streams alike. The fix for that is to request 2M only after hop authentication (when the node is known) instead of at subscription; it changes the setup order of every GATT link, so it needs its own design and a preservation run before coding. (c) The stream sender has no in-attempt 1M retry (the call has one): the recovery is the viewer's re-request, so a first failure costs about 20 s. (d) The ASUS keeps a dead incoming call link for about 11 s; a callee-side stall rule would risk closing slow healthy callers and has no evidence yet. (e) CoC streams are not covered. (f) Only a connection timeout or a heartbeat timeout is evidence; a sender closed by its owner after a crawl (`GATT input interrupted`) is not recorded.

**Phone plan (A15s source and ASUS viewer; one owner for both; frozen APK from `fix/a15s-sender`; same placement; each arm 3 runs of 90 s; stream 640x360@15, 300 kbps, Automatic; `test_reset_learned_state` before every run unless the arm says otherwise; unfiltered logcat on both and the HCI snoop pulled for every failed run).** A control, `test_stream_adaptive_phy=false`: expect status 8 within 5 to 18 s of the PHY update in every attempt (this reproduces d8c7589c). B fix, switches default: expect the first link to fail with `adaptive_phy: link=stream ... early_loss=true` and the next link to log `reason=remembered | at=connect` and `PHY TX 1M / RX 1M`, with 60 s or more of continuous reception on the ASUS and no status 8; record the time from start to first rendered frame and the 1M goodput against the 300 kbps request (the requested rate is not changed to pass). C 1M baseline, `test_ble_phy=1m`: same pair, to separate 1M capacity from the memory logic. D split arms, `test_gatt_phy_split=tx2m-rx1m` then `tx1m-rx2m`, with `test_stream_adaptive_phy=false`: the observed `PHY TX/RX` line must show the asymmetric pair (if the controller refuses it the arm is unmeasured), then count status 8 and time to loss; the arm that survives names the working direction. E calls: A15s calls ASUS, three consecutive calls after a reset, switch default versus `test_stall_remembers_2m=false`: expect call 1 to stall or fail, call 2 to start on 1M (`reason=remembered`) and connect. F preservation: ASUS to A15s stream and call, A15s to M31 stream, S24 and M31 pairs and a two-viewer fan-out source: no `link=stream ... early_loss=true` line, 2M kept, and the same results as the last frozen build. G the HCI snoop of one control run through `tools/btsnoop_summary.py`: per-direction ACL gaps, LL PHY and connection-update ordering, which side stops acknowledging.

**Change impact (AGENTS).** 2026-10-13, branch `fix/a15s-sender`. Changed: `StreamPhyLoss.kt` (new), `BluetoothVideoSender.kt` (loss probe, heartbeat message constant), `GattVideoTransport.kt` (first disconnect status exposed, per-direction PHY request), `BlePhyPreference.kt` (`GattPhySplit`, `phyRequestMasks`), `AdaptivePhy.kt` (`advertisedPeerToRemember`), `AndroidPrivateVideoCall.kt` (memory at the stall), `DebugTunables.kt` and `MainActivity.kt` (three switches and the `le_link_policy` line). Affected scope: every direct GATT stream source on every phone (a new log line and, after an early 2M loss, 1M for that peer), every LE call dial that hits the stall guard, and nothing else (no wire change, PHY request unchanged while the split is off). Cases to retest: GATT stream and call cases with an A15s or any phone as source or caller, preservation of healthy 2M pairs.

#### A15s as a sender, phone results of build 326aed2c and the PHY policy question (Q-PHY1, 2026-10-13)

**What the runs show.**

| Arm (A15s source to ASUS viewer, GATT, 640x360@15 requested, 90 s) | Result |
| --- | --- |
| A, `test_stream_adaptive_phy=false`, 2M/2M | 3 of 3 fail (status 8, redial on 2M each time) |
| B1, defaults, memory reset | 7 attempts in 90 s, none recorded (see "Gap 1"), never streamed |
| B2 / B3 | first link 2M lost (`early_loss=true`, silent 7.8 s after authentication), then `remembered | at=connect`, 1M/1M, 1243 and 1318 frames, 0 freezes |
| C, `test_ble_phy=1m`, 1M/1M | 3 of 3 clean, TX 163 to 179 kbps (the encoder's rate, not the link's limit) |
| D, `tx1m-rx2m` (observed `TX 1M / RX 2M`) | 3 of 3 clean, 175 to 179 kbps |
| D, `tx2m-rx1m` (observed `TX 2M / RX 1M`) | 2 of 3 clean at 150 to 180 kbps; D2 started at 62 kbps (write p99 753 ms), the one-PDU rule moved it to 1M after 11 s, the 8 s backlog was never recovered (29 s queue, 75 percent frozen) |
| E calls A15s to ASUS, defaults | 3 of 3 set up: call 1 stall guard at 3.005 s, memory written at the stall (`reason=setup_stall`), 1M retry found the ASUS after 3.7 s, ACTIVE; calls 2 and 3 started on 1M (`remembered`), hop authentication 179 to 232 ms |
| E calls, `test_stall_remembers_2m=false` | call 1 as above (memory only after the retry), call 2 passed the guard on 2M (hop 451 ms) and died within 13 s of going active (write timeout, 96.5 percent frozen, then early-loss memory), call 3 stall and retry |

**What D says.** The A15s controller needs 2M in both directions at once to fail: every symmetric 2M link died or crawled (9 attempts in d8c7589c, the three A runs, B1), 5 of 6 links with 2M in one direction only were clean, 3 of 3 on 1M were clean. The asymmetric pair is really established by the stack (the observed PHY lines show `TX 2M / RX 1M` and `TX 1M / RX 2M`, so the arm is measured). The weak element is therefore not one direction of the radio but the A15s MediaTek controller's handling of 2M: the earlier snoop (`AdaptivePhyPolicy`, 2026-10-09) showed it completing exactly one ACL packet per 15 ms event on 2M against four on 1M, and these runs add that the symmetric case also loses the link, and that its 2M receive path is slow under load (below). A 2M transmit that works two times in three is not a reason to keep 2M for the A15s transmit: 1M carried everything the encoder produced.

**F preservation is not a regression.**
- ASUS to A15s stream (reset, defaults): the ASUS source (central) wrote 48 to 74 kbps on 2M against the 300 kbps request, the backlog started only when the encoder ramp passed the link rate, the one-PDU rule fired at 31.8 s (`reason=one_pdu_per_event`, bad-2M recorded for the A15s, line present in `F-stream-asus-a15s/asus.log`) and the link then ran 92 to 157 kbps on 1M with a 6.7 s queue that drained only to 4.5 s in the remaining 60 s (noloss policy, auto ladder off). The d8c7589c run with 0 freezes in this direction (`b2 stream-asus-a15s-control-r1`) started from a remembered 1M (`reason=remembered | at=connect`, 51 to 144 kbps, queue below 700 ms); the cold run in the same folder (`stream-asus-a15s-cold-r1`) shows the same 2M picture (85 kbps, queue 7.4 s, move after 10 s). So the A15s as peripheral receiving a heavy flow is also poor on 2M, and the cost of finding out is a 20 s detection plus a backlog.
- ASUS to A15s call: the "1 frame" is the first `Private call visible continuity` line (1.3 s after start). The call ran 60 s on 1M/1M (`remembered`, hop authentication 209 ms): the A15s received 912 frames with 0 freezes, the ASUS received 640 frames with 12 freezes (8.4 percent), which is the known A15s sending limit (libaom at 82 to 130 kbps, frame-rate limit 10 FPS, DECISIONS "Call head-of-line"). It is not a 1M failure and not caused by the stall memory (guardMs=0, no stall).

**Gap 1: B1 was not caught, and why.** Of the 7 B1 attempts (one had a status 133 connect retry inside it), 3 ended with status 8 while the hop handshake was still running ("Authenticating the BLE GATT link" with no "final video receiver" line, 5.1 to 6.6 s after opening) and 3 ended by the broadcaster's 12 s connection timeout with the end-to-end handshake unfinished (hop authentication itself took 2.5 to 6.9 s, healthy is 1 s); 1 was still running at the end. The first version only watched links that had authenticated and ended in status 8 or a heartbeat timeout, so none of the setup failures counted. Now: the loss probe exists from the moment the GATT link opens; a connection timeout counts from the link opening when the hop peer never authenticated (keyed by the advertised prefix, not for a rotating pseudonym), and a link closed unfinished at least 5 s after it opened (`StreamPhyLoss.SETUP_STALL_MS`, the stream version of the 3 s call guard; healthy streams connect in about 2 s) counts as a stalled setup even though the owner closed it. A quick user cancel does not.

**Q-PHY1: start on 1M and go to 2M after authentication, remember after failure, or a pair/family policy.** The data is one pair (A15s MediaTek x ASUS Qualcomm) in both roles, plus the older snoop of the same pair and an A15s with an M31 central that carried 6 to 10 packets per event on 2M.
- *Remember after failure* (what is built). It always works for the second link and costs the first link 5 to 20 s. It cannot work with rotating discovery IDs, which are the default in user builds: the lookup before connecting uses the advertised prefix, which is a pseudonym, while entries are keyed by the authenticated node. It also leaves the slow-but-alive case (ASUS to A15s) to the 20 s one-PDU detector.
- *1M first, 2M after authentication* (built as the Experimental `test_phy_after_auth`, stream sender only). The lookup moves to after authentication, so rotating IDs work; the hop handshake runs on 1M, which removes the pre-authentication stalls of Gap 1; a healthy pair pays one PHY update about 1 s after connecting. It does not remove the first failure for a new bad pair (the 2M switch still follows, then the end-to-end handshake and media are exposed), so it needs the memory and the new setup-stall rule to recover, and a watchdog that reverts a live link to 1M was not built (reverting needs the dead direction to carry the answer; unknown).
- *Pair or family policy.* Not justified yet: D shows the A15s controller is the common factor, but the entry needs sources and data from a second phone of the family (AGENTS) and the A15s with an M31 central was healthy on 2M, so a model-wide "A15s uses 1M" would cost a working pair. Record the candidate (`mt6765` OPPO, Android 10, platform stack; behaviour: GATT video starts on 1M; confidence low; evidence b6, b8, snoop 2026-10-09) in the registry as a proposed entry with no effect until a second MediaTek phone or an official source supports it.
Recommendation: ship remember-after-failure with the setup-stall rule as the default (done), run `test_phy_after_auth` as the A/B arm now, and promote it only if (a) it removes the first-link failure or the 20 s detection on the A15s pair, (b) healthy pairs (S24, M31, ASUS streams) show no loss against the connect-time request (first frame, 2M kept after the update, no extra stalls), and (c) the rotating-ID lookup is shown to work. Promote the family entry only with outside evidence.

**Changes in this branch.** `StreamPhyLoss.kt` (setup-stall cause, window from link opening, unknown-peer rule), `BluetoothVideoSender.kt` (probe at link open, connected flag, `phy_after_auth`), `GattVideoTransport.kt` (`deferPhy`, `GattVideoConnection.requestPhy`), `BlePhyPreference.kt` (`phyAfterAuth`), `DebugTunables.kt`, `MainActivity.kt` (`test_phy_after_auth`, default off), `StreamPhyLossTest.kt` (the B1 attempts replayed). Affected scope: direct GATT stream sources (a source that sees a stalled 2M setup now remembers the peer); with the new switch off nothing else changes.

**Rerun plan (same pair, frozen APK from `fix/a15s-phy-gaps`, memory reset before each run, 90 s, unfiltered logcat both phones).** (1) B again, 3 runs, defaults: expect `adaptive_phy: link=stream ... early_loss=true` within the first 15 s in every run that fails its first link (including `cause=setup_timeout` or a status 8 with `hopAuthenticated=false`), the next link `remembered`, 1M/1M, and a rendered stream within 40 s of start. (2) `test_phy_after_auth=true`, 3 runs: expect `phy_after_auth ... requested=true` after authentication, hop authentication below 1.5 s, then either a working 2M link or the early-loss line and a 1M redial; log first-frame time and frames per arm. (3) Same arm on S24 to M31 and ASUS to M31 streams: 2M must still be reached (`PHY TX 2M / RX 2M` within 3 s of authentication) and frames/freezes must match the connect-time arm. (4) ASUS to A15s stream, reset, defaults and `test_phy_after_auth=true`: the A15s as receiver; compare time to the 1M move and freezes with F. (5) Unchanged: C and D can be skipped.

#### A15s as a sender: the alive-but-stalled 2M link (b10-97a95ad4 defaults run d2, 2026-10-13)

**What happened.** Hop authentication took 0.84 s on 2M, the end-to-end handshake finished 0.6 s later, the encoder started at +1.2 s. Then the A15s wrote 7 to 13 kbps for the whole 90 s: the writer blocked 99 percent of every window (`flush p95 3.6 s`, `frame-call p99 4.5 s`), the sender queue grew to 2,850 records (65 s of video), and the ASUS showed 10 frames and then a frozen picture. The link itself stayed up: 95 authenticated heartbeats from the ASUS arrived (about one per second), so there was no supervision timeout, no heartbeat timeout and no early-loss line. The A15s transmit direction on 2M was almost dead while the receive direction was fine.

**Why nothing fired.** (1) The early-loss rule needs the link to end (connection timeout, heartbeat timeout or a setup the owner closes); this link never ended. (2) The one-PDU-per-event rule did evaluate it after 18 s of backlog and returned `kept | kbps=7 | blocked=0.99`: `AdaptivePhyPolicy` deliberately keeps 2M when the mean is under a quarter of one PDU per event ("far below one PDU per event is a stalled link", added for mesh4-7a6c401c 0001, an ASUS relay's downstream link at 8 kbps while it was a peripheral upstream) and it decides only once per link, so the stall was never judged again. The exemption was written for a relay's scheduling collision; here the sender is a plain single-link source. Only d2 of the b10 runs ended this way (every other 2M link either died, setup-stalled, or sat at 87 to 90 kbps and moved).

**What "BLE GATT source slots full; viewer discovery stopped" meant.** Only that the source's admitted fan-out is one child (`Source fan-out: 1 children from measured BLE GATT: probe 734 kbps`) and that child had just authenticated, so the broadcaster stopped advertising for more viewers (it restarts when the slot frees). It is a normal line (it appears in every authenticated single-child stream) and was not related to the stall.

**Fix (`test_stalled_2m_move`, default on; control arm `=false`).** The policy now reports that case as `STALLED` instead of `KEEP`, and `AdaptivePhyLink` decides: a stalled writer on a link that is not a relay's own (`RelayUpstream` plus `relayPhyGuard`, the original mesh4-7a6c401c case) and whose peer is still heard (the inbound pipe received any record within 4 s, `GattBytePipe.heardWithin`) moves that link to 1M and records the peer bad on 2M (`reason=stalled_one_way`), exactly like the one-PDU move. A relay's links and a link the peer is not heard on keep 2M and remember nothing (a dead link belongs to the timeouts). Replay: `AdaptivePhyPolicyTest` feeds the d2 writer profile (7 kbps then 9 kbps, 99 percent blocked, 15 ms interval) and expects one `STALLED`; the 40 kbps band still moves as a one-PDU link; the relay, silent-peer and control cases keep.

**Recommendation, not implemented: drop the backlog built on the bad PHY at a PHY move?** For a live stream I recommend yes, once, at the move, and only when the queue is already older than the viewer can use. At the move the queued records were produced for a link that is gone; in F the queue was 6.7 s and drained to only 4.5 s in the next 60 s (fixed 300 kbps over a 90 to 157 kbps link), 63 to 69 percent of the run frozen, and in d2 it was 65 s: content that old is not live video, and the viewer's playout window (about 2 s) can never absorb it, so keeping it buys no continuity, only delay on top of the freeze. The user's no-frame-loss preference is about not discarding frames to hit a latency target while the viewer can still show them; a one-time catch-up skip when the backlog exceeds roughly twice the viewer playout (about 4 s), to the next keyframe (request one), loses frames that were already useless and then runs at the new link rate. It is no substitute for rate control: a fixed-rate stream above the link's rate grows its queue again at the excess rate, so the skip should log a notice and the continuous answer stays Automatic rate control (the fixed-rate runs are a test artefact). I would ship it as an Experimental, default-off switch (`media age` family), A/B it on the ASUS to A15s stream (F and b10 3-asus-src), and judge it by frozen percentage and the age of the first frame after the move, with the loss of the skipped frames reported next to it; calls already have the bounded-age experiment and need a separate decision.

**Rerun plan.** Same pair, frozen APK from `fix/a15s-stalled-2m`, `test_reset_learned_state` before each run, 90 s, unfiltered logcat: (1) defaults, 5 runs: any run whose A15s writer stalls on 2M must show `adaptive_phy: link=gatt ... reason=stalled_one_way` about 10 to 20 s after the first frame (3 windows after warm-up), `PHY TX 1M / RX 1M` and TX recovering to 150 kbps or more; compare frozen percent with d2. (2) `test_stalled_2m_move=false`, 3 runs: the control must reproduce `kept` when the stall occurs. (3) Preservation: S24 to M31 and ASUS to M31 streams and a two-viewer fan-out must show no `stalled_one_way` line; a relay chain (S24, ASUS, A15s) must still show `relay_scheduling` or no move at the relay.

#### A15s as a sender, the backlog skip at a PHY move (Experimental, 2026-10-13)

**What it does** (`test_phy_move_skip_backlog`; default on for fixed-rate streams since 2026-10-14, off with Auto quality, SWITCHES). When a live stream sender's own link moves from 2M to 1M (`AdaptivePhyLink` after `one_pdu_per_event` or `stalled_one_way`; a relay's moves and the relay forwarder never call it), `skipBacklogAfterPhyMove` compares the age of the oldest queued video with `PhyMoveSkip.thresholdMs(viewerPlayout)` = max(4 s, 2 x (assumed 250 ms base + the delay the viewer reported)). Above it, `shedAtomically` removes the queued video with the existing codec-safe plan: everything before the newest keyframe that is still inside the viewer's playout, or all queued video when none is, plus dropping arriving inter frames until a keyframe arrives (re-requested every 1.5 s through the same path as the bounded-age experiment, at most 15 s). Audio, codec configuration, rotation and lifecycle records are never touched, and calls and audio-only live never skip. Line: `BENCHMARK phy_move_skip: role=stream_tx | skipped=true | skippedRecords= | skippedAgeMs= | thresholdMs= | viewerPlayoutMs= | awaitKeyFrame= | queueAfter=`; `skipped=false` is logged when the switch is on and the queue is younger than the threshold. Tests: `PhyMoveSkipTest` replays the 6.7 s backlog (all video dropped, audio and configuration kept, a fresh keyframe becomes the resume point) on a real queue.

**What it is not.** It does not change the rate: a fixed-rate stream above the new link's rate grows its queue again at the excess (the F and 3-asus-src runs are fixed 300 kbps over a 90 to 157 kbps link). Automatic rate control stays the continuous answer; a viewer that hears the skip as a jump is the price for not showing seven seconds old video.

**A/B plan.** ASUS source to A15s viewer, GATT, stream 640x360@15 fixed 300 kbps (the F and b10 `3-asus-src` cases), frozen APK, `test_reset_learned_state` before each run so the 2M start and the move recur, 90 s, three runs per arm, same placement, alternating arms: control (switch absent) against `test_phy_move_skip_backlog=true`; add a third pair of arms with the stream under Automatic rate control to separate the skip from rate control. Read: the `adaptive_phy ... to=1M` line (move time), `phy_move_skip` (skipped records and age, awaitKeyFrame), the viewer's frozen percentage, longest stall and the age of the first frame after the move (media-age lines `videoQueueAgeNowMs`), and the sender queue age at 30, 60 and 90 s. Pass reading: frozen percentage falls and queue age after the move stays under the threshold, with the number of skipped frames reported beside it. A run in which the move does not happen proves nothing about the skip (log `skipped` absent). Preservation: S24 to M31 and ASUS to M31 streams, a relay chain and a call with the switch on show no `phy_move_skip: skipped=true` line.

#### Audio chain ASUS to M31 (relay) to A15s: not a regression of the guard, a dead anchor followed by a stale dial (2026-10-13)

**Question.** Did the A15s's hop stage get slower (2M setup, stream PHY memory, setup-stall logic, `phy_after_auth` plumbing) or does the guard see audio-only children differently? **Neither.**
- No commit since the passing builds touches `ReceiverStreamForwarder.kt`, `RelayChildStallGuard.kt` or `RelayEstablishFallback.kt`; the `GattVideoClient.open` changes (deferPhy, hooks) leave the request sequence identical while the new switches are off, and the logs show the same order as the passing run (priority, MTU, services, CCCD, PHY request after the 15 ms update).
- The guard measures inbound bytes from the child since the link opened, for audio-only children as for video; its `stage=hop silentMs=2005` means the A15s sent nothing after the M31's hop hello. In the passing run the hop reply took 369 ms (`handshakeMs=369 maxSilentMs=180`).
- The link was dead in both directions from the 15 ms connection update, the signature the guard was written for: music run, M31 central to A15s peripheral: update to 15 ms at 51.19 (M31 clock), PHY request 3 ms later, nothing else; the A15s logs its last reception at 51.39 and its own connection timeout 5.16 s later (56.55), and "GATT sender disconnected (end of stream)" is that timeout, not a close by the M31 (the M31 closed at 53.23, its terminate never arrived). Voice run: update at 17.65, A15s timeout 22.78. b5: the same two lines. That is the Exynos 9611 child anchor landing in a dead part of the parent's 15 ms cycle (relayfix-0725a213: 9 of 55 attempts). The passing builds simply did not draw a dead anchor; three failing chains out of three do not prove a changed rate (two passing, three failing, same pair, random anchor phase), and nothing in the logs shows the stack or the app changing the phase.

**What turns one dead anchor into a failed chain** (this is the defect we own).
1. The guard closes the link at 2.0 s and counts the stall as establishment failure 1 (by design, `isRelayStallEstablishmentFailure`).
2. The child still holds the dead link for about 3 s more, then restarts: its receiver advertising set starts and stops within 6 ms (music: `adv_set_churn start` and `stop` at 56.628 and 56.634 on the A15s) and its route cooldown (8 to 13 s) begins. The relay's scanner sees that single advertisement 3.2 s after the close and dials it.
3. Both dials (the open retries once) end in a 5 s `status 133` (the receiver is gone). The relay counts failure 2 and logs `relay_parent_fallback ... action=withdraw_offer | failures=2 | markMs=300000`; every later request of the child gets `skip_connect` for 5 minutes, so the chain never forms (the A15s's 20 s retries for 10 minutes).
The voice run reached failure 2 differently (two quick 133s while the A15s application was only 4 s old, then a connected link with a dead anchor), so this fix does not cover that start; see "Not fixed".

**Fix (`test_relay_stall_holdoff`, default on).** `RelayStallHoldoff`: when the guard closes a child link the relay records the time; sightings of that child within 10 s (the child's route cooldown less the time its dead link needs to expire) are skipped with `relay_child_stall: action=holdoff | remainingMs=` and are not counted. A successful end-to-end handshake clears it. The child's real next request dials a fresh anchor phase (about 84 percent of attempts). `RelayStallHoldoffTest` replays the music timeline: without the hold-off the stall plus the first stale dial mark the child for 5 minutes and the real request at +17 s is refused; with it the sighting at +3.2 s is skipped, only the stall is counted, and the request at +17 s dials.

**Not fixed.** (a) A dead anchor itself: the guard shortens it from 5.9 s to 2 s and the retry redraws the anchor; `test_relay_reanchor` and `test_relay_upstream_dck` remain the experiments for avoiding it. (b) The voice-run start: the relay's first two dials failed at once (status 133 after 354 ms and 1,078 ms) 4 s after the A15s application started and 0.7 s after its first advertisement; a relay should not count a failure of the first dials to a child whose advertisement is under a second old, but there is no measurement of what the A15s stack needs at that time, so it is not changed. (c) The A15s was relaunched cold for every chain case in these runs, which is unlike a user's phone; the rerun below keeps the A15s application running.

**Rerun plan.** Same chain (ASUS source, M31 relay, A15s leaf, GATT, Voice then Music), frozen APK from this branch, A15s started at least 60 s before the M31 and ASUS, 10 min each, three runs per preset.
