| R3-4 (F4, C1) | `K_cx` recovery after a crash, reset or re-enable; `CONTACT` into a void | `PENDING` and `CONFIRMED` keys with pairing epochs; a phone with the switch on answers `PAIR` even if it holds a key; a `PENDING` key is promoted only by a valid peer `CONTACT` tag; reset increments the epoch; `K_cx` is stated to prove only "the peer opted in", not mutual verification; the stale-`CONTACT` disclosure is accepted and stated; fresh mutual proof in the same session before any `HOST` or dial |
| R3-5 (F5) | The host cannot count a guest's failed dials; paging rate | `DECLINE` reason 6 (dial failed or timed out); the host counts failures (3 per address per hour); guest dials at most once per 30 s per contact |
| R3-6 (F6, C7) | `HANG_UP` ack and teardown; ownership | No ack for `HANG_UP`: sealed on the current carrier plus `CLOSE` (no sequence, so the fenced carrier can carry it) on every live carrier; microphone, camera and screen released at once; writers drain in the background; wait for all readers or 3 s as today; one closing coordinator and one finalizer; a second hang-up while ending is benign |
| R3-7 (F7, C6) | The writer is one synchronized sequence per link; `SWITCH_END` must be in-band | One ordered writer per carrier, control lanes served between records, `SWITCH_END` written in-band after the last pre-boundary record, passed through the receiver's same ordered inbound queue; a stalled Classic write is cut by a watchdog and the `RESUME` goes on the standby carrier's own writer |
| R3-8 (F8) | Reports, per-carrier liveness, fate sharing | `RECV_REPORT` kind on the standby link; per-carrier liveness timeout (today one shared `HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_NS`); fate sharing (any failure returns both directions to LE); "direction failed" defined |
| R3-9 (C4) | Fallback breaks fragmented call video | Boundaries lie on frame boundaries; at `RESUME` or a gap the receiver discards the partial assembly and suppresses fragments until index 0 of a new frame, the sender starts a new frame; strict checks unchanged elsewhere |
| R3-10 (C5) | Old carrier dies before `SWITCH_END` | `SWITCH_COMMIT` over the authenticated Classic link opens the gate and declares the gap; controls in the gap are re-sealed |
| R3-11 (F10) | Honest refactor scope | See the implementation map: writer facade, bearer events instead of call failure, `finish()` closing the second carrier, transport-keyed state re-derived, split handshake API (build, hash, write), per-type length check before allocation, exporter captured before key erasure (`SecureBluetoothLink.kt` around line 286), stream paths as separate work |
| R3-12 (F11) | First-record rule when `SETUP`, `BDO1` and `ECHO` all apply | Order `BDO1`, `SETUP`, `ECHO`; `ECHO` is omitted whenever `SETUP` applies, so the existing rules win |
| R3-13 (wording) | "Never stored for anyone else" | The text now says this phone's own address goes only to that contact; the existing address book keeps authenticated peers' addresses locally as today |
| R3-14 (C2) | A typo naming a responsive device exposes the guest's address | Q-CX12 decided by the user: typed twice, format check, last four digits shown; the residual is accepted and disclosed in PRIVACY; the test plan includes a wrong address that belongs to a responsive device |
| R3-15 (F9) | Frame-size caps | Receiver hold caps in frames, records and bytes (above); boundary chosen at a frame boundary |


| # | Finding | Resolution |
| --- | --- | --- |
| R4-1 (C1) | A proof is not ownership: a malicious contact echoes a false address X, then dials the real address Y and X becomes "proven" | The proof is scoped to the contact (`provenFor(C)`); other contacts are sent only the typed address; corroboration by several contacts is rejected (colluders corroborate each other); a phone without a typed address serves only contacts whose votes or proofs it holds |
| R4-2 (C2, N1) | Epoch divergence: A confirms the new key while B keeps the old confirmed key and lets the pending key expire | A `CONTACT` tag that verifies under no held key triggers `PAIR` even when a confirmed key exists (once per session, three per contact per 24 h); epochs are 64-bit, monotonic, and a jump above 1,000 is ignored |
| R4-4 (C4) | Liveness after a commit | `RECV_REPORT` rides the Classic link when no standby is left; Classic has its own per-carrier heartbeat timeout; no `RESUME` to a dead carrier; the report carries sent watermarks beside the opened ones, so "the peer is sending" is checkable |
| R4-9 (N2) | `HANG_UP` content and `CLOSE` order | Either order, the first wins, the second is benign |
| R4-10 (N3) | Per-link reader limits | The carrier facade copies per-link limits such as `maxCiphertextBytes` (`EndToEndVideoSecurity.kt`) to the new reader |


| # | Finding | Resolution |
| --- | --- | --- |
| R5-8 (root) | The design kept sprouting interleaving holes | Simplification principle adopted. Removed: 7 of 17 `BCX1` kinds (`SWITCH_ACK`, `SWITCH_END`, `SWITCH_COMMIT`, `FALLBACK_REQ`, `RECV_REPORT`, `CTRL_ACK`, `COMMIT_REQ`; 10 remain); the sender states `SWITCH_SENT` and `NEW` and the receiver states `ARMED` and the proposed awaiting state (from 3 + 4 states to 2 + 2: `SEND_ON` and `ACCEPT_FROM` over `O` and `N`); 19 table rows (10 sender, 9 receiver) became 9 (3 sender, 6 receiver); the cutover epoch; `oldLinkMs`, the 600 ms cancel, the 3 s ack timer, the 300 ms keyframe wait, the 1 s `COMMIT_REQ` and `FALLBACK_REQ` timers and the `peerDelivery.queueMs()` gate; the API change (opener returns a sequence) and the `CTRL_ACK` machinery. Added: the defensive stale-drop rule, a fixed 1 s and 5 MiB hold, and the stage-0 model test |
| R5-9 (root) | Verify the cutover by test, not prose | Implementation stage 0: a pure Kotlin state machine with a JVM model-checking test (exhaustive bounded and randomized interleavings of arrival on both carriers, delay and reorder between carriers, either carrier dying at any step, `SWITCH`/`RESUME`/hold timing, simultaneous directions) with six invariants, as the gate before any app wiring (T-CX-M below and PROTOCOL "Implementation stage 0") |

**Stage 0 model findings 2026-10-14 (T-CX-M ran against the pure machine of PROTOCOL "Implementation stage 0"; the round 6 text had four defects found by the model, then review 6 added three more rules; each is fixed in the PROTOCOL draft and in the machine).** The search covers both directions switching at once; either carrier dying at any step and noticed by the two phones up to 2 s apart (the half-open case); a record delayed up to two ticks behind a record on the other carrier, so that `SWITCH` trails the first new-carrier records past the 1 s hold; a writer queue on the old link that a `SWITCH` may or may not overtake; reader threads separate from the single consumer, with carrier-up and carrier-died events in the same ordered queue as the records; indispensable-state changes at arbitrary times; a second upgrade attempt after the first one died; and, beyond the transport contract, duplicated and dropped records. Checked at every step: the six invariants of the design plus a seventh (nothing is written to a carrier this phone already closed) and the rule that no policy close takes the last live carrier; at quiescence: state equal on both sides and `SEND_ON` and `ACCEPT_FROM` naming the same live carrier (or both phones ended because every carrier died); and an eighth for runs without fault, duplicate or drop: a normal switch loses no media record.

| # | Defect in the round 6 text | Smallest counterexample the model found | Invariant | Fix |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S0-1 | The hold's expiry or overflow closed `N`. When `O` had died silently and the `SWITCH` died with it, the receiver still believed `O` alive for up to 2 s, held the `N` records for 1 s and then closed the last live carrier | 5 steps: the attempt is ready, `O` dies as the sender switches (the `SWITCH` written on `O` is lost), `N` records arrive and wait out the hold, the receiver closes `N`; both phones end the session | 1 (a policy close took the last live carrier; the session ended while `N` was alive until then) | Expiry or overflow takes `N`: `ACCEPT_FROM = N` at the opener's next sequence, held records released in order, fragment suppress mode, keyframe request. No rule closes a carrier except to clean up one the peer reported dead. A late `SWITCH` changes nothing; the `O` tail below the boundary is stale and dropped |
| S0-2 | `O` lost while `N` was still in `BIND`/`READY` ended the session although `N` was about to become the carrier | 2 steps: the attempt starts, `O` dies, the first phone to notice ends the session | 1 | `O` lost during the handshake: write and open nothing, wait for the attempt (ceiling 5 s from accept, the PROTOCOL deadline table; review 6 corrected my earlier 10 s), apply the `O`-dies rows at `READY`; the attempt's timeout ends the session |
| S0-3 | `SWITCH` and `RESUME` named no carrier instance. After the first attempt died, a `SWITCH` or `RESUME` still queued on a slow `O` was applied to the second attempt: the receiver took `N2` while the sender still wrote on `O` (every `O` record fenced, a black hole), and the stale `RESUME` then closed the healthy `N2`. Reachable only when a marker waits on `O` longer than the spacing of attempts (the model used a 4 s cooldown and stalls up to 3 s); with 30 s spacing and the 2 s write watchdog it is latent, and the fix removes that dependency on timer ratios | random seed 27889 of the fixed list, shrunk from 674 to 270 steps (the directed test `theFirstDraftAppliedAStaleSwitchToTheLaterAttempt...` shows the same misapplication in six calls): attempt 1 switches and dies, the `SWITCH` and `RESUME` wait behind a stalled `O`, attempt 2 is ready before they arrive | 4 and 5 (the state differs on the two phones; `ACCEPT_FROM` names `N2`, `SEND_ON` names `O`) | `SWITCH` and `RESUME` carry the 8-byte upgrade ticket of the attempt they are about (`BCX1` kinds 8 and 9); a receiver acts only for its current `N` or the attempt still in `READY`, otherwise it burns the generation. Review 6: a marker that carries no ticket (the machine's `ANY`) is burned, never acted on; directed test |
| S0-4 | `SWITCH` can overtake the peer's `READY`: it arrives on `O` while this phone's attempt is still in `BIND`/`READY`. The round 6 rows burned it, so the early `N` records waited out the whole hold and, in the first draft, then closed `N` | found while building the model (directed test); no invariant fails, the cost is a 1 s stall or a needless fallback | none | keep `(ticket, boundary)` and apply it when `READY` completes |
| S0-5 (review 6, item 1, major) | A normal switch dropped the old link's backlog. The draft served control lanes before media lanes, so `SWITCH` could overtake the records queued on `O`; the receiver took `N` at the boundary, the `N` records overtook the backlog, and the backlog arrived stale | 4 steps (shrunk from 19, found after 500 states): the attempt is ready, a media record waits on `O`, the sender switches with `SWITCH` ahead of it, the `N` record is opened, then the queued `O` record is dropped as stale | 8 | `SWITCH` goes through the old link's ordinary media FIFO (PROTOCOL `BCX1` text exempts it from control-before-media). The owner injects `oldQueueAgeMs`, and no normal switch starts while the oldest queued record has waited half the hold (500 ms) or more; the switch also waits for the next frame start (`atFrameBoundary`). The dropped `O` tail at a normal switch becomes a measured loss in T-CX4 (target zero) |
| S0-6 (review 6, item 3) | `RESUME.next` was zeroed on return to `O`, so nothing bounded what the old link may deliver afterwards | review reading; directed test | none found | `next` is kept as the floor: after a `RESUME` the old link delivers only records from `next` on |

**Review 6 changes that are not defects.** The upgrade attempt ceiling is 5 s everywhere (code, PROTOCOL liveness paragraph, S0-2), matching the deadline table. The old `RequestKeyframe` is split: `ForceLocalKeyframe` (this phone's own encoder, sender rows) and `RequestPeerKeyframe` (the receiver rows), with a new `ResetSendFragments` output for the unsent fragments of the frame being sent. Carrier-up and carrier-died events pass through the single consumer, in reader order relative to that carrier's content (PROTOCOL "One inbound consumer"); the model now enqueues them with the records. Two half-open reductions, both measured below: (a) a `SWITCH` that arrives on `N` while `O` is still alive here proves the sender closed `O`, so this phone closes `O` too, before the repeat check; (b) an `N` that dies while `O` lives always sends `RESUME(ticket)`, and the receiver closes the named `N` even when it is not (yet) accepting it. The round 6 behaviours of all of these stay switchable in `ClassicCutover.Config` (`asFirstDraft()` turns every correction back).

**What passed (corrected rules).** Twelve bounded exhaustive searches (state-hashed depth-first over 1 s ticks, the PROTOCOL ratios of hold 1 s to detection up to 2 s, a record delayed up to two ticks, a hold ceiling of three records) finished with no violation and 13.0 million distinct states in total: switch, hold and fallback with one fault (0.63 M states), the same with the reader threads separated from the consumer and carrier events in the queue (2.13 M), two faults in either order on both carriers (1.85 M), the handshake still pending with `READY` at any time (0.77 M), a second attempt after the first died (2.07 M), indispensable-state changes at any time (2.99 M), duplicated records (0.89 M) and dropped records (1.61 M; safety invariants only, because a stream carrier does not drop records and convergence is not claimed after one), and four fault-free searches of the normal switch with a writer backlog on the old link (invariant 8: frames of one and of two records, FIFO without the gate, and the reader queue; 40 thousand states, hold 2 s and records delayed at most one tick). Coverage is asserted: they reach the old link dying with the new one up, `SWITCH` written on the new carrier, the old-link hint closing `O`, a `RESUME` notice before any switch, a `RESUME` closing a carrier that was not yet accepted, hold promotion, fallback, a `SWITCH` kept for a pending attempt and applied at `READY`, a `SWITCH` burned for a dead attempt, records dropped as fenced, stale and from a closed carrier, the switch waiting for the old link's queue and for a frame start, the attempt timeout and a legitimate end of the session. 20,000 seeded random runs (seeds 1 to 20000, real constants of 1 s hold, 3 s activity, 5 s attempt ceiling, 4 s cooldown instead of 30 s so that a second attempt fits, 15 s of 100 ms ticks, 150 media records of 1 KB to 3 MB per direction, up to three faults, detection delays from 0 to the 2 s maximum per phone, delivery stalls up to 3 s, up to four state changes per phone, two attempts, reader queue on) found no violation: 13,796 runs switched, 5,977 wrote a `RESUME`, 472 reached the hold ceiling (the queue-age gate keeps most backlogs from reaching it), 8,486 ended the session legitimately because every carrier died. 5,000 more seeds each with duplicated and with dropped records (safety invariants only) found none. The old behaviour is caught again with the corrections switched off: hold expiry closing `N` after 513 states (5 steps), `O` lost during the handshake after 79,771 states (2 steps), `SWITCH` overtaking the old link's backlog after 500 states (4 steps, invariant 8), unnamed markers from a random run (seed 27889 of the fixed list, shrunk from 674 to 270 steps). A mutation check (13 deliberate bugs in the machine, run outside the repository) was killed 13 times: 11 by an invariant or a test, 2 (the queue-age gate and the frame-start wait) only by the coverage assertion, because FIFO alone already keeps the switch lossless below the hold, so those two are guaranteed to be reached, not shown necessary; in the first check the mutant that made RESUME ignore its ticket survived and stays as defence in depth. Test cost: 19 model tests and 25 directed tests, the model class takes about 110 s in Gradle on the development laptop (exhaustive searches about 85 s, the 20,000 seeds about 12 s).

**Half-open loss, measured (the review question, restated after review 6).** One media record per 50 ms in each direction, one carrier dies, and the two phones notice it at once or after the 2 s maximum (every combination of: the old link or the new one dying, before or after the switch, the peer's own direction switched or left on the old link; no network delay, so the numbers are the cutover's own). **Round 6 text:** the longest gap the receiver sees is the sender's own detection delay plus one 50 ms record, **2,050 ms (40 of 600 records lost in that direction at 20 per second, about 40 audio records or 60 frames at 30 fps)**, whenever the sender notices late, whether or not the peer noticed at once. That is the figure for a dead carrier with an empty writer queue; the honest statement is **2,050 ms plus the dead carrier's queued backlog** (measured with 250 ms still queued when the carrier dies: 2,350 ms). **Corrected text, with reductions (a) and (b):** both phones notice at once 50 ms (0 records lost); one phone notices at once 100 ms (1 record lost); neither notices before 2 s 2,050 ms (40 lost, plus the backlog). Neither reduction alone lowers the worst case of the middle row (round 6 text, (a) alone, (b) alone: 2,050 ms each): (a) acts when `O` dies and (b) when `N` dies, and the worst case runs over both. What remains is the 2 s liveness timeout when neither phone notices early.

**What the model does not show.** Records are opaque (no media, no fragments: only the existence of the suppress and reset outputs is checked); the writer is a FIFO per carrier whose only reordering is the first draft's priority `SWITCH`, and a record the writer never sent when its carrier dies is modelled as a write into a dead socket or, in the backlog measurement, as discarded in flight; the machine never looks at record kinds; time is coarse (1 s ticks in the exhaustive searches, 100 ms in the random runs, 50 ms in the half-open measurement; the real 3 s, 30 s and 5 s constants run in the random searches only, with the cooldown shortened as stated); the owner is assumed to call `onCarrierPending` when the new socket connects, never to start an attempt while another is alive, to map the 8-byte ticket to a per-session attempt counter, and to call the machine only from the consumer, with carrier events in reader order; the two phones run the same machine. Invariant 8 holds only while no record is delayed beyond the hold and is checked in fault-free runs; with longer delays the hold promotes by design and the old link's tail is dropped (the gate makes that rare, T-CX4 measures it). S0-3 and the stale-record drop depend on timing the model does not enforce: with a 2 s write watchdog and 30 s dial spacing a marker cannot outlive an attempt, so the ticket is defence in depth for `RESUME` and a real guard only if that timing ever changes.

**Phone tests and gates (not run).** Same gates for every phone; eligibility follows capabilities (Classic RFCOMM and the switches). S24, M31, ASUS and A15s now; S22, Xiaomi and A55 when they return. Frozen APK, switches by launch extra. The two phones of a pair are verified contacts with a confirmed key (a `PAIR` session first); no Android bond may exist at the start (bonds removed; address books, claim store, votes and the typed address cleared; the echo log is the evidence because Samsung masks its own address in `dumpsys`).

| Test | Setup | Pass when |
| --- | --- | --- |
| T-CX0 off-preservation | Everything off, the usual call and stream pair matrix | Link hello mask bit 5 clear, no hop type 14, no upgrade listener, no visibility request, results within noise of the previous build |
| T-CX1 first contact and compatibility gate | Never-met contacts with a key, no bond: S24 <-> M31 and ASUS <-> A15s, both setup roles; one phone has its typed address entered twice | Pairing dialogs, bonds and visibility prompts are counted and **reported, not assumed**: pass needs zero visibility prompts, zero pairing dialogs and zero persistent bonds (a temporary link-layer pairing is logged separately); a pair that shows a dialog or a bond fails the gate on that stack and stays on LE. The typed address is confirmed by an echo or a bind within 30 s of the Classic connect. Wrong-address cases: (a) an address that nothing answers fails 3 dials and the user is told; (b) an address that belongs to a **responsive nearby device** (a third phone or earbuds): the dial is recorded, the disclosure of the dialer's address to that device is noted as the accepted residual, the node ID check rejects it, `DECLINE` reason 6 reaches the host and no media is affected |
| T-CX2 seeded contact | A seeded host and a never-met contact guest | Zero prompts on stacks that pass T-CX1; media on Classic within 15 s of the answer (median), 25 s (worst of 5) |
| T-CX3 pair matrix | Every ordered pair of the four phones that pass T-CX1, both call setup roles, 5 repeats, calls then streams | At least 90 % of attempts reach Classic; every failure falls back with no gap above 1 s; time from answer to first Classic media (median, p95) |
| T-CX4 cutover quality | Calls and streams at a fixed requested rate through the upgrade window, 5 repeats per pair, including a congested LE link (a queue of 500 ms) and an idle sender | Interruption at the receiver (`pauseMs`) has a target of 200 ms (p95 at most 200 ms, maximum at most 350 ms, provisional); the idle-sender and congested cases still complete the cutover without cancelling; old-link delivery gap no worse than baseline p95 plus 250 ms; no freeze above 1 s caused by the switch; at most one keyframe request; zero endpoint opener rejections; no lifecycle or signal record lost across the cutover; the old-link tail dropped at a normal switch (records, bytes and milliseconds of media sealed before the boundary that the receiver never opened) is measured and reported, target zero, and no switch starts while the old link's oldest queued record has waited 500 ms or more |
| T-CX5 throughput | Fixed requested rates 150, 300, 600 and 1,000 kbps as separate cases; GATT-only arm against upgrade arm; 60 s after the switch | Classic arm delivers at least 99 % of the offered load at every rate where the GATT arm cannot, at least twice the GATT arm's sustained ceiling for one rate; at the lower rates latency p95 and frozen share not worse (thresholds provisional); the requested rate is never raised |
| T-CX-M stage-0 model test (JVM, no phone) | The pure cutover machine: exhaustive bounded interleavings and seeded random runs; both directions switching at once; arrival on `O` and `N` delayed and reordered between carriers; either carrier dying at any step on either side; `SWITCH`, `RESUME` and hold timings; duplicated and dropped records | **Passed 2026-10-14 on the corrected rules ("Stage 0 model findings"; eight invariants there).** All six invariants hold: the session never ends while a carrier lives; the opener never receives a record that is not above the last opened one; delivered records are a strictly increasing subsequence of the sealed ones; indispensable state is equal on both sides after quiescence; no deadlock (bounded ticks bring `SEND_ON` and `ACCEPT_FROM` to the same live carrier); a closed carrier never delivers. **No app wiring starts before this passes** |
| T-CX6 failure injection on phones | Each `test_classic_upgrade_inject` value, plus Bluetooth off/on on the guest after the switch, a blocked Classic write, simultaneous cutovers in both directions, `O` lost before, during and after the switch, seen by the sender only and by the receiver only, a delayed `RESUME`, `N` closed by the receiver | The call or stream continues on whichever carrier lives; abort within 25 s; both directions use `O` again within 3 s of an `N` failure; after `O` is lost `N` carries both directions and its liveness works without LE; a stalled outgoing path is not hidden by incoming heartbeats; the opener never rejects a record at a cutover; no leaked listener or UUID |
| T-CX7 controls and fragments | `HANG_UP`, a lifecycle change, a stream end, a microphone and a camera toggle sent during the cutover, a forced `N` loss and `test_call_video_fragments=true` | The call ends within 5 s of the tap on both phones with one finalizer run; a simultaneous hang-up from both ends is benign; lifecycle, microphone and camera states converge to the latest value after the re-assertion and no late one reverts a newer; no old ciphertext is replayed (fresh sequences in the log); no fragment-reassembly failure ends the call (suppress mode resyncs only at offset 0; a lifecycle record during suppress mode does not end the call) |
| T-CX8 privacy and security | A stranger, an unverified peer, a one-sided contact (verified one way, no key) and a contact with a key; rotating IDs on and off; wrong `ECHO` and wrong `HOST` injected; a malicious contact voting its own device; a crashed or reset key side; a third phone connects without a valid proof, then replays a recorded proof | Strangers, unverified and one-sided peers receive no `BCX1` record except an opted-in `PAIR`, and are never dialed even when they send `HOST`; adverts byte-identical between switch off and on (scanner-side capture); a wrong echo is one vote and the selected address stays; the malicious contact's address is never offered to another contact; a one-sided key recovers by `PAIR` without a stale `CONTACT` followed by `HOST`; a contact that echoes a false address and then dials the real one gets no offer of the false address for other contacts; a `CONTACT` that fails under every held key triggers one `PAIR` and the keys converge (`pending_expire`); a wrong `HOST` is dropped after one dial and never enters the address book; the stranger socket closes within 2 s with no handshake byte sent; the replayed proof fails |
| T-CX9 endurance | 10-minute call and 10-minute stream on Classic, then a forced Classic loss | Revert to LE; after the session no upgrade listener or extra connection remains; the standby's radio cost (battery, connection events) recorded for the standby decision |

Logs: `BENCHMARK classic_upgrade: event=pair|contact|echo|self_address|host|listen|dial|bind|ready|switch|switch_on_n|resume|hold_expired|reassert|close|revert|abort | requested= | effective= | address=...XX:XX | source=typed|echo|proven | gen= | msFromAnswer= | pauseMs= | reason=`. Each T-CX also reads the rendered-frame cadence and the audio gap report; an offered-load check precedes any rate comparison.



### Design COC-01 (proposed, not built): fixed-PSM LE CoC without link encryption (Android 16)

**Status.** A draft from the device-family research; **needs a design review and a discovery/wire change before any
code**. Nothing in the app uses it. Default off and Experimental if it is ever built.

**What Android 16 adds.** `BluetoothSocketSettings` lets a listener ask for an LE CoC socket **without** link-layer
encryption or authentication and on a **fixed PSM in the dynamic range 128-255** (aosp-bt-versions COC-01). Today a
server takes whatever PSM the stack allocates (round-robin over those 128 values, shared with every app) and publishes it
in a GATT characteristic that the client must read before it can open the channel, after which the "GATT first, CoC on a
fresh ACL" sequence is needed (the stack drops the ACL about 1 s after the last GATT client closes, CONN-08/COC-05).

**What it would remove for an Android 16 pair.** The GATT PSM read and the control link behind it, the fresh-ACL timing
dance, and one connection setup round (about 1-3 s of setup and a source of 133 failures). An unencrypted LE CoC is not a
loss of confidentiality here: MeshLive's own handshake and AES-256-GCM records already protect every byte, and the
Android-level link key is not relied on (PRIVACY "No authenticated radio link on LE").

**What it needs (why it is not a drop-in).** (1) Discovery must say that a phone listens on the fixed PSM without a
GATT control service, a capability bit in the announcement or the first record, which touches the uniform adverts (Design
E) and the interoperability rules for older phones and iPhones, so a pair uses it only when both ends advertise it. (2) A
fixed PSM is a collision risk: another app may hold it, so the listener needs a defined fallback to the dynamic PSM and
the GATT read. (3) Rotating IDs and the address-privacy rules must still hold (the connect target is the same rotating
address). (4) Android 16 only, so a wire-compatible fallback to today's flow stays mandatory. (5) Phone tests: connect
time and failure rate with and without the GATT read, on the Android 16 phones (S24 and others) and against the stack's
socket-exception codes.

**Open questions for the review.** Does a capability bit leak anything a scanner can use (it is one more linkable
capability bit, PRIVACY)? Is the PSM fixed per app version or negotiated per pair? Does an unencrypted CoC change what an
active attacker can do beyond dropping and jamming (it should not, since records are sealed end to end)? Should the first
build be a measured A/B only, behind a DEBUG switch, before any protocol bit is assigned?

### Design SG (Option A coded 2026-10-15 behind `coc_auto_gatt` + `test_coc_auto_gatt_streams`, off; phone tests not run): automatic GATT fallback for streams when a receiver cannot open an LE CoC listener


**Design review required** (security and wire sections below). Default off; it would ship as an Experimental sub-switch
that needs `coc_auto_gatt` (Settings > Features "Switch to Bluetooth LE (GATT) when L2CAP fails") and has its own DEBUG
extra (`test_coc_auto_gatt_streams`). Today `coc_auto_gatt` covers private calls only.

**Problem.** A receiver on a stack that stops accepting LE CoC listens (the Android 10 A15s leak: about 15 registrations
per Bluetooth process, closing sockets does not free them) cannot start a stream receive over BLE L2CAP at all. For calls
the phone detects the failed listen and restarts on GATT (`onCocFailure(AVAILABILITY_FAILED)`), and its offer is listed by
GATT-aware callers. For streams nothing happens: the receiver reports a startup failure and stays unreachable. The fix is
not symmetric, because a stream receiver is not a call endpoint: it advertises a viewer request and the source dials it.

**Evidence (code and logs).**
- The receiver's listen failure is the `STARTUP` stop cause in `AndroidVideoReceiver` (any exception before or while
  accepting); `MainActivity` then reports a startup notice and has no transport retry. The call path retries through
  `CocRegistrations.session.onCocFailure(...)` (`CocAutoGatt.kt`).
- The source selects viewers with `announcement.transportOption() == outgoingTransport` (the viewer-request scan filter in
  `startViewerRequestDiscovery`). A GATT viewer request (announcement flags `0x09`: accepting plus GATT, PROTOCOL's
  version 5 vector) is therefore invisible to an L2CAP source, so a receiver that restarted on GATT alone would never be
  dialed.
- `BluetoothVideoBroadcaster.connect(...)` takes one session-wide transport, which feeds admission (`fanOutChildren`,
  `admissionTransport`), `LinkCapacity.budgetKbps(transport, FAN_OUT, perLink)` and the starvation withdrawal.
  `BluetoothVideoSender.connect(...)` already takes the transport per child, so a mixed fan-out is possible at the link
  level; the planning and the encoder rate are what is session-wide.
- Call evidence that the mechanism works where it exists: with `coc_auto_gatt` on, the A15s rang and went active over GATT
  in 22/22 calls, and 0/22 with it off.
- A GATT link carries a lower sustained rate than LE CoC (`BluetoothTransportOption.maxVideoBitrateKbps` differs by
  transport), so a GATT child cannot take the stream of a CoC-rate source unchanged.
- The default-on `CocListenerHub` (one long-lived listener per role) already removes most of the registration pressure, so
  the fallback is a safety net for the case where a listen still fails.

**Options.**

| Option | What happens | Gain | Cost / risk |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **A (recommended). Receiver falls back; the source adds a separate GATT child pool (mixed fan-out)** | The receiver restarts on GATT after a `STARTUP` failure on L2CAP (once per adapter session, same ledger as calls, visible notice naming requested and effective transport). A source whose own transport is L2CAP and which has the sub-switch on also accepts GATT viewer requests, dials them as GATT children with their own pool (initially 1) and charges them to the aggregate budget at their measured GATT per-link rate. Never the reverse: a GATT or Classic source never accepts L2CAP children. | The fallen-back phone receives; other children, the encoder request and the requested transport of the session are untouched; reuses the per-child transport parameter and the signed link hello. | Mixed-transport admission and accounting; a GATT child cannot take the CoC bitrate, so it needs a thinned layer or the lowest layer (Design RT relay child thinning, `layerShareLabel`) or it is refused with a visible reason; shared radio (a GATT child costs airtime of the CoC children). |
| B. Source restarts the whole session on GATT when a viewer can only do GATT | One transport per session, as today. | Simple admission. | Every healthy viewer is cut down to GATT rates and the session restarts; it changes a requested transport mid-run (against the benchmark rule: never silently change a requested transport). Acceptable only when the source has no other child; not recommended as the general rule. |
| C. Do nothing on the air; tell the user | A clear receiver notice: this phone's Bluetooth cannot receive L2CAP streams; turn Bluetooth off and on (a 20-25 s toggle restored L2CAP for one cycle). | No protocol or admission change, no new radio mix. | The phone stays out of every L2CAP stream until the user acts; weak for the off-grid user who cannot diagnose it. |
| D. Source advertises dual transport (Design D for streams) | The source hosts a GATT server beside its CoC server and says so in a hint bit. | Reuses the Design D idea. | Wrong direction for streams: the viewer is the advertiser and the source is the central, so the source needs a GATT client, not a server; the hint adds a linkable bit to stream offers (PRIVACY). Rejected. |

**Recommendation: A, in stages, with C's notice shipped first.** Stage 0 is the receiver notice (plus a
`stream_listen_failed` BENCHMARK line carrying the stop cause and the CoC registration count), which costs nothing and needs
no review. Stage 1 is the receiver-side restart on GATT behind the sub-switch with the source unchanged, tested only to
prove that a GATT viewer request appears and that the flag-off path is byte-identical. Stage 2 is the source's separate GATT
child pool; it is the part that needs this record's review and the phone tests below. A is preferred over B because B turns
one phone's stack bug into every viewer's quality loss.

**Wire and PROTOCOL impact.** None on the air for stages 0 and 1. A GATT viewer request is already encodable (flags
`0x09`); no new bit, field or UUID. Stage 2 changes only the source's local filter: with the sub-switch on and an L2CAP
source, a viewer request with `FLAG_GATT_TRANSPORT` and an accepted stream token is also selected, and the child link is
opened by the existing GATT path with the existing signed link hello (which already carries the transport bit), so the
hello bytes of every existing case stay identical. PROTOCOL gets one sentence in its viewer-request section stating the
source filter rule and that the transport of a child link is the transport of the request that selected it. The
rotating-ID rule (a version 5 viewer request mirrors the offer's version) is unchanged: the request is built by the same
code, with the GATT flag.

**Security and authentication (nothing is relaxed).**
- Hop authentication and ordered AES-256-GCM records are the same on a GATT child as on any hop; the link hello and the
  end-to-end stream identity checks (authenticated original source and requested parent, stream token binding) run
  unchanged. A fallen-back child is a child like any other: admission, the starvation guard and overload withdrawal apply,
  and a direct fallback to the source from a requested relay chain is still a failed chain (no silent route change).
- No downgrade by a third party: the fallback is decided locally by the receiver from its own failed listen (never from a
  received advertisement), and the source's acceptance of GATT requests requires the same stream-token window as an L2CAP
  request. A nearby attacker can at most request a GATT slot, which is bounded by the separate pool, the token check and
  the authentication that follows; it cannot raise the pool or lower another child's transport.
- Privacy: no new announcement bit and no address change; the GATT viewer request is the existing form, so the discovery
  rules (Design E, Design F) hold. The notice and the BENCHMARK lines name transports, never identities.

**Phone tests (not run; a design is not a result).** The A15s (Android 10) is the leak phone and is not one of the six
required phones; required-phone coverage uses a DEBUG listen-failure injection (`test_coc_listen_inject=stream`, to be
added with stage 1, off by default) on a healthy stack, so the S22, M31, ASUS, Xiaomi and A55 can run it.
1. Off-preservation: sub-switch off, the same launches produce the same viewer-request scan filter, admission lines and
   stream bytes as the frozen baseline build, on S24 to M31 and S24 to ASUS (L2CAP) and on a GATT source.
2. Receiver fallback: injection or real leak on the receiver; within the normal join time plus one GATT setup the receiver
   restarts on GATT with `requested=BLE_L2CAP effective=BLE_GATT reason=availability_failed` logged once; with the
   sub-switch off the failure is unchanged.
3. Mixed fan-out: an L2CAP source with one healthy L2CAP child and one fallen-back GATT child: both receive; the healthy
   child's throughput, frame age and loss stay within the noise of the same source without the GATT child (same settings,
   same APK, three repeats); admission lines show two pools and the aggregate charge; the GATT child gets a layer it can
   carry, or a visible refusal.
4. Refusals: GATT slots full; the GATT child cannot carry the lowest layer; a second GATT request. Each is visible and
   nothing is substituted.
5. Topologies: the fallen-back phone as a direct child, as a relay's child, and as a relay (its own children over L2CAP
   from a GATT parent), checking the authenticated original source and requested parent; a requested chain that falls
   back to direct is recorded as failed.
6. Recovery: Bluetooth off/on on the receiver returns it to L2CAP on the next join (the ledger and forced reason reset).
7. Concurrency: the call matrix pairs (both directions) with a call and a stream at once on the fallen-back phone, since
   GATT servers and clients share the app-wide GATT slot pool.

**Open points for the review.** The initial GATT pool size and the aggregate charge rule are conservative guesses until a
mixed run is measured; whether thinning is available for a direct child of a source (it exists for relays) decides if
stage 2 ships with the lowest-layer rule only; a viewer that is already a relay child of a GATT parent is stage 2 work.

## Plan detail moved from the plan (2026-10-12)

The longer descriptions of the policy workstreams, intercom work, discovery-privacy phone-test plan, headsets, distribution, iPhone interoperability, Wi-Fi bearer, missing measurement adapters and platform work. The plan keeps one short item for each.

### Policy work: fewer fixed limits

Fixed numbers (1100/800/700/500/400 kbps ceilings, 20% margins, 5 s keyframes) were wrong for some phone or link every
time, and fixes kept circling the same freezes. Each workstream removes a root cause instead of tuning a constant; each
step freezes a build, records what broke, what changed and the evidence in [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md), and is
compared on the same coverage cases.

| Workstream | Root cause removed | Design | Done when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| A. No frame loss (the default; under A/B against bounded media age, below) | Every congestion response dropped frames: a queue overflow dropped all queued video and waited for a recovery keyframe (the largest frame), overloading the same link again; temporal-layer thinning cost about 2.5 VMAF | Latency is the elastic resource: ordered sender and relay queues, receiver queues sized for the adaptive delay with backpressure, late media played, audio kept on the clock by a small pitch-preserving speed change; a relay child that stays far behind is withdrawn so it can reroute (hop-local), never thinned | No queue drops; freezes and audio drops at or below the previous builds at the same settings; latency inside the playback profile |
| B. Self-measured links | Link capacity differs per model, peer, Wi-Fi state and concurrent links; no API exposes it | Every large record is a packet train: the receiver times its arrival and reports the estimate on the control channel; GATT senders also time large writes; estimates feed the learned per-phone, per-peer capacity | Estimates match probe maxima within about 15% and track Wi-Fi and concurrency changes |
| C. Burst-free encoding | Keyframes of 40-136 KB need over a second of airtime on any Bluetooth link | Native AV1 caps frame size relative to the measured link; hardware encoders use intra-refresh where supported, keeping full keyframes only for joins | No scheduled frame above about 250 ms of measured link time |
| D. No fixed limits left | Picker maxima and priors are constants | The bitrate control shows the measured link estimate; priors become first measurements | Remaining constants are bounded fallbacks used only before the first measurement |

Open: the policy for calls beyond a link's capacity (lower call frame rate, longer call delay, or the current video skip); forced CBR on the M31 and the 15-20 s encoder trim, judged by same-build VMAF; relay child joins (start the child from the relay's next natural keyframe, or grow the relay's playout delay before it connects a child); replacing the 20% per-link planning margin and the 0.9 ceiling headroom. The measured status behind each workstream is in [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) under "Open-defect investigation notes".

### Intercom core and product direction

Intercom (voice, push-to-talk) and live video are equal parts of the product; the reasoning is in
[DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) under "Product direction".

| Work | Done when |
| --- | --- |
| Bounded media-age experiment (**Experimental**, default off) | Calls keep speech within about 300-400 ms by lowering bitrate or resolution, dropping codec-safe non-reference frames, then going audio-only; streams buffer up to about 2 s, then shed or resync while keeping authenticated order. A randomized A/B against no-loss (M31 <-> ASUS, S24 <-> A15s, both directions, 150-1200 kbps with bursts) reports media age, stall length, frozen share and speech continuity, and the maintainers choose the default from it. No-loss stays the default meanwhile |
| User-centred gates | The `device-run-v4-user` profile in [TESTING.md](TESTING.md) is built around media age, longest stall, frozen share, audio continuity and task success. Remaining: a decided media-age target, and adapters for task success and speech comprehension |
| Intercom core | Reliable 1:1 voice and push-to-talk in small groups, text, photos and location with time and accuracy; screen-off operation and reconnect proven on phones. Later: store-and-forward voice and video messages (Design A) |
| Signing mode | A legibility-first video profile (at least 20 FPS target for hands and faces), validated with fluent signers before any accessibility claim |

Current recommendation: keep no-loss as the default and bounded age as an off-by-default experiment. The interim evidence is in [DECISIONS.md](DECISIONS.md) under "Open-defect investigation notes".

### Discovery privacy

**Design review required.** Today a phone advertises a stable 48-bit identity prefix, so a passive scanner can follow it
between places. The goal: discovery stops broadcasting a stable prefix, using contact-scoped rotating identifiers and
explicit discoverability windows. In-person contact verification (QR code or safety code; the verification API exists
and its UI binding is still to come) and key-change warnings come before the app is positioned for at-risk users.

Status: rotating discovery IDs, the BMH3 handshake and pseudonyms toward strangers are on by default since 0.2.0-beta (an explicit stored choice wins); formats and vectors are in [PROTOCOL.md](PROTOCOL.md) and measurements in [PRIVACY.md](PRIVACY.md). Not measured: battery cost, the Bluetooth name read on the A15s, an active attacker holding a contact's key, fake adverts, relays with the new handshake and abuse limits. Open design question: the monotonic epoch never returns after a backward clock step, so a lead of 3 to 96 epochs leaves a phone unable to receive a version 5 call until it recovers. Phone-test plan:

0. **Phase 0 measurements** on every reference phone (`tools/rotid_phase0.py`): does an advertising-set restart change
   the address; are the GAP Device Name and PSM characteristic readable by an unpaired central; do SCAN_REQ and
   CONNECT_IND addresses equal a set address; which OS beacons rotate when; do established links survive a set stop.
   On iOS: does a stop and start change the iPhone address (RV17).
1. **Phone cases for phase 1:** discovery latency and call setup equal to switch-off; boundaries while idle and during
   a call; a passive-tracker scanner sees zero address or ID carry-over across boundaries (RV6); logged boundary
   offsets between phones (RV12); restarts inside an epoch keep the epoch ID (RV4).
2. **Streams and viewers on version 5 (phase 1b):** role 1 and 2 rotating IDs, stream token grace (60 s),
   admission-key translation, the recovery hint with `Khint` (RV18), forwarders and relay-chain mirroring.
3. **Wire contacts with presence leases (phase 2):** mutual verified contacts only, with a per-contact switch "Can see
   when I'm nearby" on by default (RV11); a store sealed with the verified-contacts Keystore key; an unconfirmed "seen
   nearby" line without a check mark; a new generation on Forget, Reset and wipe; no automatic connection from a match
   until BMH3.
4. **Contacts-only mode** (labelled limited until BMH3, RV13), the Visible-by-handle window for streams, iPhone
   compact v2 advertising and reach pages version 6 with naming phase 2.
5. **Separate designs:** the BMH3 identity-hiding handshake (SIGMA), uniform advertisements and a primary-PDU
   announcement (RV7).

Key-change handling and safety numbers for store-and-forward contact cards (Design A) belong here too.

### Bluetooth headsets

Wired and USB headsets are honoured: a plugged one replaces the earpiece, and the loudspeaker unless the person
explicitly chose it. Bluetooth headsets (SCO and LE Audio) are deliberately never selected as the call output, because
they take airtime from the same radio that carries the call's own Bluetooth media link. The rule is the single call
output decision (`callOutputFor` in `PrivateCallAudioSession.kt`), covered by JVM tests.

That is a protective default, not a measured limit: no headset-coexistence measurements exist yet. Remaining work:

1. Measure, per Bluetooth stack and transport, what a connected headset costs a live call (throughput, stalls, media
   age, audio continuity) with the headset idle, in A2DP, in SCO and in LE Audio, against the same call without it,
   recording the radio conditions.
2. If the cost is acceptable on some stacks, offer headset output as an explicit, labelled opt-in (default off,
   Experimental) with a visible warning, scoped by a device-family policy entry with sources and an off-switch. Never
   change the call's media-link settings to make headset output look better.
3. Keep LE Audio broadcast paths out of scope unless a public API, hardware and actual output evidence all exist (see
   "New Bluetooth and API facilities" below).

### Distribution and field reports

- **F-Droid and IzzyOnDroid.** The repository carries the metadata and recipe (`fastlane/`, `distribution/`).
  IzzyOnDroid serves developer-signed APKs from the GitHub release unchanged; F-Droid builds from source. Remaining:
  real phone screenshots in `fastlane/` and the inclusion requests (the application ID is already the final
  `io.github.meshlive.app`).
- **Release verification and field reports.** The About screen shows the app version and the signing-certificate
  fingerprint, and Export diagnostics copies the block defined in [ARCHITECTURE.md](ARCHITECTURE.md#diagnostics-block-contract-for-export-diagnostics) (no media,
  messages, contact names or full keys). The current builder is a minimal fallback; the full block must come from
  bounded in-memory records the app keeps (connection, adaptive-PHY and codec events, device-policy state), never from
  logcat: release builds strip `Log.v/d/i` (`app/proguard-release-logs.pro`), and other apps' logs are not readable.
- **Google Play** would also need Play App Signing, a privacy policy and Data safety form, and a decision on the
  Bluetooth, camera and microphone permission declarations. None is started.

### iPhone interoperability

**Design review required** for anything on the wire. Compact discovery (an iPhone-sized advertisement) is implemented
behind `test_ios_compact_discovery` (PROTOCOL.md "Experimental iPhone compact discovery"); the iOS client lives in the
sibling repository MeshLive/meshlive-ios. Done when an iPhone's call offer, viewer request and stream offer are found
and connected by every reference phone with the switch on, in both setup roles, with `scan_stats` counters and evidence
of whether each phone filters the masked UUID in its controller or falls back to host filtering (AOSP
`ScanManager.shouldUseAllPassFilter` moves a whole scanner to all-pass when it has more filters than free controller
slots; the switch doubles each scanner's slot use).

Optional H.264 is not coded (it would be default-off `test_h264_codec`). It would use `BTV3` codec 4, encode and decode
mask bit 2, `capabilitySummary` bit 2 or compact codec bit 56, and call-hello decode-mask bit 6 (`0x40`, prefer H.264);
all are free today and receivers already reject unknown codecs. Before coding, a PROTOCOL.md design and review must
settle: bit 7 (prefer VP9) wins when both preference bits are set; SPS/PPS delivery (replay the cached config before
each IDR, or set `KEY_PREPEND_HEADER_TO_SYNC_FRAMES`, with receivers accepting in-band SPS/PPS); no B-frames
(`BTV3` timestamps are monotonic); decoders accepting any supported profile, not only Constrained Baseline; Annex B
access-unit validation; and an H.264-only iPhone failing cleanly against Android builds without it.

### Wi-Fi Direct / Aware bearer (later)

**Design review required.** An optional, faster bearer that needs no infrastructure, with the same authenticated
identities and encryption, chosen explicitly. Bluetooth stays for discovery and as the fallback. Not started. It must
not become a silent transport fallback, and each bearer is measured separately under the same gates. (The experimental
Wi-Fi Direct page for sharing the app itself is a different, offline-sharing feature.)

### Measurement adapters still missing

The harness has host regressions for native input binding, marker decoding, recording import, content-ROI diagnostics,
raster calibration, sound-cue analysis, AudioTrack clock estimates, pipeline, lifecycle, resource and overlap
analysis, and controlled impairment (see [TESTING.md](TESTING.md)); none creates a phone pass by itself. What remains:

| Remaining work | Required result |
| --- | --- |
| Native stress and calibration evidence | Prepare only the native variants selected for the next comparison; characterize spatial, temporal and chroma demand and measure the selected encoder's actual time, bursts and queue growth. Validate the framing detector and tolerances at captured viewports; generated checks do not establish physical colour, optics or sufficient stress |
| Actual output capture and clocks | Verify acquisition modes, sensor and audio clock skew, exposure, rolling shutter, room path and dual-channel isolation on a real camera and microphone instrument, with independently measured cross-phone clock anchors. Decoder or Texture callbacks cannot approve physical cadence, blur or optical latency |
| Reference colour | Verify the requested range, matrix, transfer and chroma through each actual encoder and decoder |
| Acoustic experience | Complete clock coverage and diagnose timestamp and underrun changes against planned pauses. Waveform fidelity, speech quality, routing, echo and double-talk remain open; verify mute and independent audio and video phases over the full lifecycle |
| Lifecycle and trace preservation | Extend the generated-input pause, resume and End path to Camera2, foreground, lock, process and thermal cases and real remote acknowledgements; process-death reconstruction is separate product work |
| Radio and endurance | Run declared controlled stalls and loss at actual offered loads, keeping both call directions and every hop; separately measure real radio interference and range and matched 15-30 minute unplugged endurance |

Write focused tests for each adapter's failure cases before using it to close a benchmark request, and keep results in
the suite's own state rather than a second ledger.

### Platform and product work

| Area | Remaining boundary |
| --- | --- |
| Audience membership and trust (**design review required**) | Static group-key distribution exists. Dynamic revocation, epoch rotation and expiry, and rejection of old keys remain unfinished (Design B's `BGK2` covers part). Full-fingerprint verification exists and is opt-in per contact; trust-on-first-use pinning of unverified peers and an independent protocol audit remain open. Audience modes must change admission without disabling encryption |
| Branches and alternate routes | Normal viewer-forwarders support one child and one active parent. A multi-child UI, four-phone group and adversarial validation, and larger alternate-parent graphs remain open; a diamond needs loop, duplicate, TTL and hysteresis handling. Simultaneous redundant media and make-before-break recovery need separate architecture work |
| Call recovery and expansion | Screen lock, OS or Bluetooth interruption and process-death reconstruction need evidence and a bounded reconnect design. The opaque relay diagnostic builds chains of up to four relays (PROTOCOL.md "Private-call relay chains"); remaining: physical evidence for two or more relays, route discovery for ordinary users (the relay is DEBUG-only), relay forwarding counters and a measured relayed call cap (experimental `relayed_call_measured_cap`) |
| Background and session ownership | Typed foreground services protect ordinary app switching. Reconstructing camera, audio and authenticated sessions after process death needs an explicit design and lifecycle proof |
| Windows receiver | The retained desktop receiver is video-only (`BTV2`). Add current `BTV3` video and audio and ordinary discovery before claiming interoperability with current Android camera-and-audio streams; capture lifecycle and teardown need physical evidence |
| Power, range and display | Matched unplugged, role-reversed endurance, real heat, and controlled range and interference measurements; charging runs and nominal Low Energy do not establish efficiency. Replacing TextureView with SurfaceView needs measured geometry, lifecycle and power preservation |
| New Bluetooth and API facilities | Temporal-layer dropping proceeds through workstream A with bitstream-verified droppability; API 37 temporal-layer IDs or HDT only with a compatible public API, hardware and actual output evidence. HDT is not an arbitrary CIS or BIS payload socket, and hidden or root-only LE Audio paths are not product features. Classic and LE bandwidth aggregation and relay re-encoding are not implemented product paths |

These rows keep future obligations without turning them into approved implementations. When a requirement changes,
update the benchmark scope and inventory together with its reason (see [TESTING.md](TESTING.md)).
